## Celebrate This Week — full redesign, all 196 weeks, 2026-09-21, MIGRATIONS CONFIRMED LIVE

Item #6 off the 2026-09-21 action list, direct follow-on to the Care Team redesign and Mental Health Phase 2 work below, same session-arc. Roop's own redefinition of this card's job, arrived at through a real design conversation with ChatGPT that she brought back and worked through with Claude line by line before any content was written: **"Every week, Mom's Village notices something worth celebrating about her journey that she might otherwise miss."** Celebrate This Week is the one card in the Care Chart that asks nothing of her — no task, no question, no completion state, no badges/streaks — unlike every other pillar, which helps her *do* motherhood. This one exists to give her a moment to *enjoy* it.

**Three real drafting rounds before the concept itself was right, each correcting a genuine, distinct failure mode — worth remembering for any future content pass on this card:**
1. **v1 — read as pregnancy/motherhood information dressed up with a "Celebrate This Week" heading**, not genuine celebration. Roop's test, now the standing bar for this whole series: *"If the heading 'Celebrate This Week' disappeared, would the sentence itself still feel like somebody was celebrating something with her?"* Lines like "Your body already knows exactly what it's doing" are reassurance, not celebration — that job already belongs to other cards.
2. **v2 — became genuinely celebratory but leaned on manufactured justification phrases** ("worth celebrating," "worth marking," "worth a little cheer") propping up content that wasn't inherently celebratory on its own. Roop's fix: inherent celebration needs no tag — "Halfway there!" or "Six months together!" carry themselves; a development fact with "worth a cheer" stapled on the end doesn't.
3. **v3 (locked) — celebration inherent to the line itself**, via a milestone marker, an exclamation, pregnancy/parenting humour, or a genuine "look how far you've come" beat, no justification tag needed.

**Governing rules for every week in the series, confirmed and applied throughout:**
- One celebration per week, normally 1–2 sentences, no task/question/completion state.
- Never make the celebration depend on a specific milestone having happened — babies and mothers vary too much for that to be safe (Roop's explicit example: never *"Celebrate! Your baby is crawling!"* — a mother whose baby hasn't crawled yet would read that as a signal something's wrong). Any developmental/behavioural beat is hedged (possibly/probably/there's a [decent/good/real] chance), never stated as universal.
- No "you survived another week" repetition.
- Naturally vary between celebrating her, the baby, their bond, and the funny/ordinary reality of motherhood — no visible category or tone metadata shown to her, no new database table, no structured columns. This stayed a lightweight rewrite of the existing plain-text `celebrate_this_week` column throughout, per Roop's explicit instruction not to build it into a Nourish-scale content architecture.
- Any factual developmental claim used as a hook is independently verified first (cardiac activity ~6wk, fetal movement/quickening typically 18–21wk for a first pregnancy — not the popularly-quoted 16wk, fetal hearing/voice-response developing ~23–28wk) — several early draft lines (movement at 18/25, hearing at 22/27, fetal motor practice at 32) were softened or replaced rather than stated as one universal timeline.
- **Calendar/month labels are reserved for wherever this app's real week-to-month mapping deliberately defines them** (week/4.345, the same conversion established in the Nourish Week 81 lesson elsewhere in this file), never loosely estimated from a round week number. Caught and corrected repeatedly: "Four months in!" at postpartum week 19 (actually ~4.4 months, and redundant with an existing "four months almost here" a few weeks earlier), "basically the one-year mark" at postpartum week 48 (four weeks early — Roop's note: *"four weeks is quite a lot in baby time"*), "nearly two and a half years"/"nearly three years" claimed several months too early in the year-three batch. Real, defined markers used in the final locked content: 6 months = week_number 66, 9 months = week_number 79, first birthday = week_number 92, 18 months = week_number 118, second birthday = week_number 144, two and a half years = week_number 170, third birthday = week_number 196 (this project's own already-established "third birthday" marker, ~35.9 months by the strict conversion, treated as close enough per the same precedent already set in the Year Three Part 2 Care Team/closing-note work).
- **Postpartum must not lean on pregnancy's "X weeks together!" framing as a fallback** — gestational week itself is meaningful in a way that a bare postpartum week count isn't across 156 weeks, so postpartum content leans on absurd/ordinary motherhood specifics, the changing mother-baby relationship, and real calendar markers instead.
- **The postpartum series must not gradually become "look how competent you've become as a mother."** Roop's explicit correction after the first postpartum sample: several early drafts assumed she already understood her baby's cries, had settled routines, was "functioning well," felt recovered, or had mastered a skill by a specific week — all real assumption risks for a mother who's struggling. The emotional register instead rotates across her growing confidence (sometimes, never the default), the funny absurdity of life with a baby/toddler, something lovely about the relationship, the stage itself, baby's emerging personality (always hedged), ordinary family moments, real calendar milestones, how different their world has become, and an occasional genuine "look at you" moment for Mum.
- **Humour and personality material deliberately increases as the child ages into toddlerhood and the preschool years**, per Roop's explicit direction that the writing should mature with the child rather than staying in the softer newborn "bond/world/learning each other" register for all three years.

**Content — five migrations, all validated via `pglast` (196/196 rows, zero gaps, zero duplicates across the full week_number 1–196 range):**
- `supabase/migration_69_celebrate_this_week_pregnancy.sql` — weeks 1–39. Locked after the three drafting rounds above; Roop's own week 13/20/24/33/37 lines kept at or very close to her original wording.
- `supabase/migration_70_celebrate_this_week_postpartum_1of4.sql` — postpartum weeks 0–14 (week_number 40–54), the sample batch used to establish the postpartum voice. Corrected weeks 42/43/45/46/48/49/54 to remove assumed maternal competence per Roop's note above.
- `supabase/migration_71_celebrate_this_week_postpartum_2of4.sql` — postpartum weeks 15–52 (week_number 55–92), through the first birthday. Corrected weeks 55/59/65/67/74/76/85/86/87/88 for behaviour/feeding/routine assumptions and calendar-math drift (the "four months"/"basically one-year" errors) before locking.
- `supabase/migration_72_celebrate_this_week_postpartum_3of4.sql` — postpartum weeks 53–104 (week_number 93–144), through the second birthday. Humour ramps up into real toddler material (tantrums over trivia, "mine," copying Mum, throwing things for the joy of it) with consistent hedging.
- `supabase/migration_73_celebrate_this_week_postpartum_4of4.sql` — postpartum weeks 105–156 (week_number 145–196), through the third birthday, closing the series. Preschool-era material (imaginative play, a toddler's own sense of humour, early empathy, borrowed "big kid" phrases). Week 196's `celebrate_this_week` deliberately stays in this pillar's own light register rather than duplicating the fuller farewell already carried by that same row's `closing_note` column (migration_47).

No schema changes, no keepsake mechanism, no completion state anywhere in this build — exactly the lightweight scope Roop specified throughout.

**Status: all five migrations written, validated, and confirmed run in Supabase, 2026-09-21.** Roop confirmed via two direct SQL checks: a spot-check across 8 weeks (one from each migration plus the real milestone weeks) showed the correct rewritten text at every checkpoint, and `select count(*) from care_chart_week_content where celebrate_this_week ~ '(🎉|💛|😄|🥳)'` returned exactly **196** — every week across all five migrations is live, no gaps. Still needed: (1) push all five migration files via GitHub Desktop (no other files touched — this was a pure content migration, no `CareWeekContent.tsx` or other code changes needed since the card already renders whatever is in `celebrate_this_week`); (2) live-check a handful of weeks across the span (a pregnancy week, an early postpartum week, one around the first birthday, and the closing week 196) on web and/or native. Batch 4 (postpartum weeks 105–156) is live in the database per the count above, but still worth Roop's own quick read-through of the content itself at some point, same as batches 1–3 got before being called final. Item #7 (Rediscover placement) closed the same day — see the section below.

## Rediscover — pulled out of Care Chart entirely, now its own top-level pillar, 2026-09-21, PUSH PENDING

Closes item #7 of the 2026-09-21 action list, the last one open. Roop's own words on why: Rediscover "has become very big thing now," so it should "come out of the care chart completely" and become "completely an independent pillar" — not restructure any of Rediscover's own screens (those were already built as a real standalone marketplace on 2026-09-19: profiles, Showcase/Find/Collaborate/Explore, private messaging, recommendations, a disclaimer page), just fix where a mother actually *reaches* it from.

**What was actually still wrong, found by reading the code rather than assuming.** The 2026-09-19 build already pulled Rediscover's own content/schema out of Care Chart on the *web* side — `CareWeekContent.tsx`'s pointer card for it was already retired, and `DashboardNav.tsx` already listed Rediscover as its own top-level tab. What hadn't happened: (1) the **native** app still had a real pointer card for Rediscover sitting on Care's own landing screen (`mobile/app/(tabs)/care.tsx`, right below the Mental health & support card) — so on the phone, Rediscover was still something you had to go through Care to find; (2) native had no dedicated tab for it at all — it was a pushed route (`mobile/app/rediscover.tsx`) reachable only via that Care pointer card or Community's banner; (3) the **public homepage** (`src/app/page.tsx`) didn't mention Rediscover anywhere in nav, and its "Four things, always with you" pillar section and top nav's "Explore" dropdown both predated the 2026-09-19 split — the homepage's own Care Chart marketing copy (`careCards`) still listed Rediscover as one of Care's five pillars (Body/Food/Mind/Skin/Rediscover), which had been stale and inaccurate for two days.

**Two placement decisions confirmed via AskUserQuestion before touching anything:**
- **Native: Rediscover becomes a real 6th tab** in the bottom bar (Today/Care/Wealth/Library/Community/Rediscover) — not just a card on Today, and not just removed from Care with nowhere prominent to land.
- **Web: Rediscover gets its own top-level nav link**, not folded into the "Explore" dropdown that groups Wealth/Care/Library/Community — a deliberate choice to NOT treat it like one more grouped pillar, matching how big a feature it's actually become.

**Native build.** `mobile/app/rediscover.tsx` (the old pushed route) moved to `mobile/app/(tabs)/rediscover.tsx` — same screen, same logic, unchanged; only the header swapped from `DrillHeader` (a pushed-screen back-arrow header) to `ScreenHeader` (the wordmark + profile-icon header every other tab root uses), and relative import depth adjusted for the new folder location. Since Expo Router group folders like `(tabs)` don't appear in the actual URL, every existing `router.push("/rediscover")` call — including Community's own cross-promotion banner in `community.tsx` — kept working with zero changes needed there. `mobile/app/(tabs)/_layout.tsx` gained a 6th `Tabs.Screen` for it (sparkles icon, matching the icon already used for its old pointer card). The pointer card itself was removed from `care.tsx`'s landing screen entirely, along with its now-unused `rediscoverCard`/`rediscoverText` styles and the now-unused `router` import that removal left behind.

**Web build.** `src/app/page.tsx`: added Rediscover as its own `<li>` in the desktop nav (between the Explore dropdown and Pricing) and `src/components/HomeMobileNav.tsx`'s hamburger menu; added Rediscover as a 5th card in the homepage's pillar-preview section (renamed "Four things, always with you" → "Five things, always with you," grid widened to 5 columns); removed Rediscover from the stale `careCards` array describing Care Chart's pillars (now correctly 4: Body/Food/Mind/Skin, grid narrowed back to 4 columns) with an updated comment explaining why. `DashboardNav.tsx` and `CareWeekContent.tsx` needed no changes — they'd already been done correctly on 2026-09-19.

Verified clean on `npx tsc --noEmit` and `npx eslint .` (web, zero errors) and `npx tsc --noEmit` (native, zero errors) and `npx expo lint` (native — back to the standing 18-problem `react/no-unescaped-entities` baseline documented elsewhere in this file; zero new issues in `rediscover.tsx` or anywhere else touched this pass).

**Status: built and verified 2026-09-21, NOT yet pushed via GitHub Desktop.** Still needed: (1) push `mobile/app/(tabs)/rediscover.tsx` (new file), `mobile/app/rediscover.tsx` (deleted), `mobile/app/(tabs)/_layout.tsx`, `mobile/app/(tabs)/care.tsx`, `src/app/page.tsx`, `src/components/HomeMobileNav.tsx` via GitHub Desktop and let Vercel redeploy; (2) sync native to Roop's phone (`npx expo start`, reload in Expo Go) and confirm Rediscover shows as a real 6th tab with no leftover pointer inside Care; (3) live-check the homepage's new nav link and 5-pillar section on web. This closes item #7 and, with it, every item on the 2026-09-21 action list.

## Maternal Mental Health — Phase 2 (PPD + PPA), 2026-09-21, PUSH PENDING

Direct follow-on to the "For Your Care Team" redesign below, same session. Roop asked what "PPA" was (postpartum anxiety — PPD's less-discussed sibling: persistent worry/racing thoughts/physical symptoms/compulsive checking rather than low mood, at least as common as PPD in the literature but far less screened for). This led to a real design conversation, run partly through a written brainstorm with ChatGPT that Roop brought back and reviewed with Claude line by line before anything was built.

**The governing boundary, confirmed as this feature's actual product promise, Roop's words:** "Mom's Village doesn't diagnose or decide what's wrong. It helps you notice when something feels different, put it into words, involve someone you trust, and reach the right support." This is now a real, visible, standing statement inside the product (not just a design principle) — Roop's explicit ask: "mothers should know explicitly that our goal is to help them identify if they need help, not push any diagnosis on them."

**Key finding before building anything: most of the real infrastructure already existed.** This project already had, live since 2026-07-29 (Phase 1): a daily mood check-in feeding a Pattern page (non-diagnostic tiering: not enough data / mostly steady / some heavy / several heavy — `src/lib/checkinPattern.ts`), a heavy-day safety bridge (fires when mood_score ≤ 1), a Prepare-to-talk-to-someone script generator (including the one real safety-signal question, routing straight to `/safety` on yes/maybe), a Support-for-today page, and an About page. Claude flagged that ChatGPT's brief — while directionally right — proposed rebuilding a lot of this from scratch, including a brand-new weekly "Mind Check-In" (😊😐😟🆘) that would have duplicated the existing daily mood signal and split the data the Pattern page already reads. Roop agreed and explicitly dropped that piece: **"Do NOT add my proposed weekly Mind Check-In... A second mood question creates duplication and confusion. Keep one signal architecture."** The real job this pass was to strengthen and re-language the existing system, not build a second one alongside it.

**What was actually built, confirmed with Roop point by point before touching code:**

1. **NORMALISE — a late-pregnancy card, shown before she needs it.** New, unconditional (not gated by mood or check-in), shown for `week_number` 28-39 — third trimester start through birth, confirmed via AskUserQuestion over the alternative of waiting until week 34-36. Two parts: her own card, titled "A note for after birth," using Roop's own words verbatim ("Emotional changes after having a baby are common. But persistent sadness, anxiety, fear, numbness or feeling unlike yourself deserve attention too. You don't have to decide what it is. You just have to tell someone."); and a collapsible "share this with someone close to you" message she can copy and send to whoever she wants to bring into the loop — not gendered to "husband," matching the same WHO-varies principle just established for the Care Team card. Matches ACOG's own recommendation to give educational material to the mother and her support system ahead of delivery. Not stored in the database — static content, so no migration was needed for this piece.
2. **REACH — a real ready-to-send message.** The old "Reach one person" line on the Support-for-today page (previously just a suggestion to send some message) is now a real generated message ("I haven't been feeling like myself lately. I'm finding things harder than usual and I don't want to brush it off. Can I talk to you about it and can you help me get some support if I need it?") behind a "Get a message you can send" toggle, with copy-to-clipboard — confirmed via AskUserQuestion over a native share-sheet variant, since copy-to-clipboard works identically on web and native with no extra permissions. New component: `src/app/dashboard/care/mental-health/support/ReachSomeoneAction.tsx`.
3. **The hub's front door rebuilt — the single biggest change.** The old 4 entry cards ("Could this be postpartum depression or anxiety?", "Check my recent pattern," "Help me prepare to talk to someone," "Support for today") read as diagnosis-first — they require a mother to ask "what condition do I have?" before she clicks anything. Replaced with Roop's 6 first-person doors: *I feel overwhelmed* / *I feel anxious or constantly on edge* / *I feel low, numb or unlike myself* / *I'm having thoughts or feelings that scare me* / *Someone close to me is worried about me* / *I need help right now* — each routing into the exact same About/Pattern/Prepare/Support/Safety pages underneath (nothing about those pages' own logic changed). Roop's reasoning, worth keeping: "a woman may never think 'I have postpartum anxiety'... she may think 'why can't I stop imagining something happening to my baby?'" Pattern and the explicit "Postpartum depression & anxiety" education page are still reachable, just as smaller secondary links below the six doors now, rather than the main entry — so a mother who already knows the clinical terms can still find them directly, while the main door stays experience-first. The non-diagnosis statement (see above) sits at the top of this same page, above the doors.
4. **The About page split PPD and PPA into two named, explicit sections.** Previously they were folded into one merged symptom list under a single "What it can look like" heading — which is part of why Roop had to ask what PPA even was. Now: "Postpartum depression" (its own short description + symptom list) and "Postpartum anxiety" (its own short description — explicitly naming it as less-discussed and at least as common — + symptom list including physical symptoms and compulsive baby-checking, which depression-only framing had been missing entirely). "Thoughts of harming yourself or your baby" stays a separate, clearly flagged safety line, not folded into either symptom list. Postpartum psychosis section unchanged. Page heading changed from a question ("Could this be...?") to a plain, findable label: "Postpartum depression & anxiety," with "(PPD / PPA)" named directly underneath.
5. **Care Team integration — drafted, reviewed, and now migrated.** Roop and Claude agreed the Care Team redesign (see the section below) shouldn't be reopened wholesale for this — instead, 10 strategic weeks (not the full 196) get a mental-health-aware Care Team addition, varying the job by stage. Claude drafted all 10 against each week's real `theme_title` (pulled from the live migrations, not from memory) and presented them for review before writing any migration, same process used for the full Care Team redesign. Roop reviewed line by line, approved 7 of the 10 exactly as drafted, and asked for 3 specific wording edits — all three now applied and migrated (see `migration_68` below).

**`supabase/migration_68_care_team_mental_health_weeks.sql` — the 10 weeks, final content.** All 10 use the existing `for_your_care_team_who`/`_lede`/`_detail` columns from migration_67, plain-text `for_your_care_team` kept in sync as the same fallback. Week 36 (WHO: Partner or support person) — "Know this can happen. Emotional shifts after birth are common and say nothing about how she'll mother. If she seems persistently low, anxious or unlike herself after birth, check in gently and help her reach support if she wants or needs it." — Roop's edit: softened from an earlier "help her reach support without waiting for her to bring it up," which read as paternalistic for a partner to be instructed to do. Week 40 (Whoever's with her most) — "Notice her, not just the baby... ask her 'how are you, really?' more than once, genuinely" — kept as drafted, Roop flagged this one as excellent. Week 42 (Partner or support person) — "Ask again, differently... try 'what's been the hardest hour of your day?'" — kept as drafted, also flagged excellent. Week 46 (Her doctor) — "Use the check for her mind too: if emotional wellbeing doesn't come up, she can raise how she's been feeling — not only how her body is recovering." — Roop's edit: removed an earlier unverifiable assertion that the six-week check is "often the only appointment about her." Week 53 (Family or friend) — "Don't assume the fog has lifted... if she still seems flat or anxious, that assumption is worth checking" — kept as drafted. Week 66 (Partner or support person) — "Notice if 'fine' has become automatic... name it out loud together" — kept as drafted. Week 92 (Whoever's closest to her) — "A milestone isn't proof she's okay... ask her directly, not despite the celebration" — kept as drafted, also flagged excellent. Week 118 (Family or friend) — "Keep checking, even now. There's no 'by now she should be fine' deadline on this." — kept as drafted. Week 144 (Partner or support person) — "Ask her, not just about her... ask how she's doing — not how the family's been" — kept as drafted. Week 190 (Whoever's in her corner) — "Keep the door open: if she's struggled before — or something doesn't feel like her now — check in again. Support doesn't have an expiry date." — Roop's edit: replaced an earlier "if something was ever mentioned as more than an ordinary hard day..." framing, which read as vague and implied the app kept a mental-health record on her. Validated via `pglast`: 10 statements, zero parse errors.

**Roop's explicit review verdict, preserved verbatim since it's the actual governing sign-off:** "Claude has implemented the architecture we agreed on without creating a second mental-health system, and the separation between education, support, professional preparation, and urgent safety is clear... Those are short, memorable, and teach the people around her how to notice rather than turning them into amateur diagnosticians." She also reconfirmed, unprompted, that the About page's PPD/PPA symptom lists must stay framed as "things a mother may notice and may want to discuss with a professional, never as criteria by which Mom's Village tells her she has PPD or PPA" — confirmed this already matches what was built (each list sits under a description, never phrased as diagnostic criteria).

**Clinical review — a deliberate, bounded flag, not a blanket claim.** Roop pushed back on Claude's instinct to say "independent primary-source verification is sufficient forever": her call is that for the prototype stage, sourcing against ACOG/WHO/NIH (as already practiced throughout this project) is right, but before a real public launch at scale, this specific safety-critical pathway — trigger wording → escalation → emergency resources → PPD/PPA education → prepare-for-doctor script — should go through a real Indian perinatal mental-health professional's review, not the whole app. Recorded here as a pre-launch action item, distinct from and not requiring the ongoing per-content verification this project already does everywhere else. If these specific strings are edited later, they should go back through that same review once it's happened.

**Code — web.** `src/components/CareWeekContent.tsx`: new `PregnancyMentalHealthNormalise` component, gated on `week.week_number >= 28 && week.week_number <= 39`, rendered right after the "what you may notice" block. `src/app/dashboard/care/mental-health/page.tsx`: `CHOICES` renamed `DOORS` (6 entries), new non-diagnosis statement box, secondary links row (Pattern + About) below the door grid. `src/app/dashboard/care/mental-health/about/page.tsx`: split PPD/PPA sections, relabeled heading. `src/app/dashboard/care/mental-health/support/page.tsx` + new `ReachSomeoneAction.tsx`: real ready-to-send message.

**Code — native.** Native has no dedicated Mental Health hub screens at all — the hub/about/pattern/prepare/support pages are web-only, reached via `Linking.openURL` out to the live site (same "don't duplicate a standing page into the app bundle" precedent already used for the Rediscover disclaimer and legal pages). So only the NORMALISE card needed a real native port: `mobile/app/(tabs)/care.tsx` gained a `PregnancyMentalHealthNormalise` component (same gate, same verbatim text, same collapsible share message) using the new `expo-clipboard` dependency (`~8.0.8`, confirmed Expo-Go-safe via `bundledNativeModules.json` before adding) in place of `navigator.clipboard`. New styles (`normaliseCard`/`normaliseEyebrow`/`normaliseToggle`/`normaliseShareBox`) added to `mobile/constants/theme.ts`'s `care.tsx`-local styles, reusing `moduleCard(Colors.terracotta)`.

Verified clean on `npx tsc --noEmit` and `npx eslint` (web, zero issues) and `npx tsc --noEmit` (native — checked against a locally-stubbed `expo-clipboard` type declaration since this sandbox's network proxy blocks the npm registry, same category of workaround already documented elsewhere in this file; the stub lives only in the gitignored `node_modules` and is replaced by the real package the moment Roop's own `npm install` runs) and `npx expo lint` (native — 18 problems, confirmed identical to the standing pre-existing `react/no-unescaped-entities` baseline, zero new issues; every touched/new line in `care.tsx` checked directly against the flagged line numbers).

**Status: built and verified 2026-09-21, migration_68 written and validated — NOT yet run in Supabase, NOT yet pushed via GitHub Desktop.** Still needed: (1) run `migration_68_care_team_mental_health_weeks.sql` in Supabase's SQL editor; (2) run `npm install` in `mobile/` (new `expo-clipboard` dependency) before `npx expo start`; (3) push `src/components/CareWeekContent.tsx`, `src/app/dashboard/care/mental-health/page.tsx`, `src/app/dashboard/care/mental-health/about/page.tsx`, `src/app/dashboard/care/mental-health/support/page.tsx`, `src/app/dashboard/care/mental-health/support/ReachSomeoneAction.tsx`, `mobile/app/(tabs)/care.tsx`, `mobile/package.json`, and `supabase/migration_68_care_team_mental_health_weeks.sql` via GitHub Desktop and let Vercel redeploy; (4) sync native to Roop's phone; (5) live-check the NORMALISE card on a pregnancy week 28-39, the new 6-door hub front end, and the 10 mental-health-aware Care Team weeks (36, 40, 42, 46, 53, 66, 92, 118, 144, 190), on both platforms. This closes the PPD/PPA Phase 2 redesign — per Roop's explicit call, no further feature should be added on top of it; the next real step for this pathway specifically is the bounded pre-public-launch clinical review flagged above, not new build work.

## "For Your Care Team" — full redesign, all 196 weeks, 2026-09-21, MIGRATION + PUSH PENDING

Closes item #3 of the 2026-09-21 action list. Direct follow-on to the second-person audit below, same live-review session. Roop's own words on what was wrong: the card had drifted into "what to tell/ask your doctor" — sometimes useful in pregnancy, increasingly repetitive later, and by year three it had mostly collapsed into "No specific ask this week" (94 of 196 weeks were this exact placeholder). But a card literally named "For Your Care Team" should mean a care team much broader than clinicians.

**Roop's redefinition, her own words, now the governing definition:** "For Your Care Team" = one small, timely thing the people around her can do this week to make her life safer, easier or better. The care team varies week to week — partner, family, friend, doctor, nurse/ASHA/ANM, lactation support, physiotherapist, counsellor, employer, childcare support, whoever is actually relevant. Every card answers: **"Who could help me this week — and what exactly can I ask them to do?"** Deliberately not "things your husband should do" — Roop moved the WHO tag between partner/support person, family, friend, caregiver and professional across the whole 196-week set on purpose, so the feature teaches building and using a village over three years, not defaulting to one relationship.

**Process, confirmed via AskUserQuestion before any of it was built:** presented three structural options for redefining this card; Roop picked "rename + restructure into a short prompt list," then interrupted before that got built with "let me redesign and redefine it myself" — she wanted to write the actual content, not have Claude algorithmically restructure the existing 196 sentences. Asked for and got a document of the current live "for your care team" text for all 196 weeks (extracted programmatically from the 11 relevant migrations via a state-machine SQL tokenizer — same technique used for validating jsonb blocks elsewhere in this project) to review against. Came back with a small approved sample (16 weeks spanning pregnancy through year three, a WHO tag + one bold instruction + concrete detail, tone shifted off "ask/confirm/contact") — she confirmed this mechanism was right, then delivered her own complete 196-week content brief in the same format, written by her against the real theme_title of every single week.

**Two structural clean-ups, confirmed with Roop before writing content, then carried through:** 7 weeks (131, 136, 152, 159, 176, 188, 192) were just navigation pointers to another pillar (Wealth planner, a Library book, the Community forum) instead of a real card — those pillars are already promoted elsewhere in the app, so removing the pointer loses nothing. 1 week (147, "Potty Training, At Your Own Pace") had drifted into child-developmental clinical guidance (a pediatrician referral about the *child*) rather than supporting the mother through her people. Both categories were rewritten into real Care Team cards, per Roop's explicit instruction, rather than left as-is.

**Claude's one contribution to the content itself: a full final alignment pass, per Roop's explicit ask ("do one final pass against the exact theme/content of each corresponding week before putting it live").** Every one of the 196 cards was checked against that week's real `theme_title`/`journey` text (pulled fresh from the live migrations, not from memory). 190 of 196 lined up tightly already — Roop had gone through the real theme list herself before writing these, and it showed. 6 read as generic relative to that week's more specific theme and were tightened to reference it directly, keeping her voice and WHO choice throughout: week 79 ("Nine months — as long as pregnancy" — now names the milestone explicitly rather than a generic invisible-load line), week 99 ("When your toddler won't let go," separation anxiety — now specifically about who does the goodbye/handover), week 116 ("Feeding a family, sustainably" — now about owning a repeatable dinner rotation, not a generic energy-planning line), week 141 ("Toddler Safety, Next Phase" — now a room-by-room safety walk-through together), week 149 ("Preschool or Not Preschool" — now the actual joint decision, not generic parenting-support language), week 183 ("Screens and Boundaries, Settled" — now ties to holding that settled household rhythm together). No other content changes were made to Roop's brief.

**Schema — `supabase/migration_67_care_team_redesign.sql`.** Three new nullable columns on `care_chart_week_content`: `for_your_care_team_who`, `for_your_care_team_lede`, `for_your_care_team_detail` — so the card can render a visible WHO tag plus one bold short instruction and the concrete detail, matching the approved sample exactly. `for_your_care_team` itself is kept and kept in sync as a plain-text fallback (`"{who} — {lede}: {detail}"`), same superseded-but-kept convention as `your_corner`/`care_for_yourself` before it — nothing reading the old column breaks. Roop's six internal drafting buckets (SHARE/REST/NOTICE/SPEAK/PROFESSIONAL/PLAN) were editorial machinery only, per her explicit instruction — not stored anywhere, not shown to the mother. 196 plain `UPDATE ... where week_number = N` statements, one per week — idempotent, safe to rerun. Validated via `pglast` before handoff: 197 statements (1 `ALTER TABLE` + 196 `UPDATE`s), zero parse errors; spot-checked the trickiest embedded-quote rows (weeks 45, 56, 174) directly in the generated SQL to confirm escaping held.

**Code.** `src/components/CareWeekContent.tsx` (web) and `mobile/app/(tabs)/care.tsx` (native): both widened their `care_chart_week_content` select to include the three new columns, and both now render the WHO tag as a small label beside "For your care team" (native reuses the existing `moduleEyebrow` style, newly imported), with the lede in bold followed by the detail in regular text — falling back to the plain `for_your_care_team` string if the structured fields are ever null (a week not yet covered by this migration, or a future content gap). Verified clean on `npx tsc --noEmit` and `npx eslint` (web, zero issues) and `npx tsc --noEmit` and `npx expo lint` (native — 18 problems, confirmed identical to the standing pre-existing `react/no-unescaped-entities` baseline documented elsewhere in this file, zero new issues; the three touched lines in `care.tsx` sit nowhere near any of the 18 flagged lines).

**Status: built and verified 2026-09-21, migration confirmed run in Supabase — NOT yet pushed via GitHub Desktop.** Still needed: (1) push `src/components/CareWeekContent.tsx`, `src/app/dashboard/care/chart/page.tsx`, `mobile/app/(tabs)/care.tsx` via GitHub Desktop and let Vercel redeploy; (2) sync native to Roop's phone (`npx expo start`, reload in Expo Go); (3) live-check the WHO tag + bold lede render correctly on both platforms, spanning at least one pregnancy week, one early-postpartum week, and one later-postpartum week. This closes item #3 of the 2026-09-21 action list — item #4 (PPD/PPA real focus under Mental Health & Support) is now built too, see the section above this one. Items #6 (Celebrate this week rotation) and #7 (Rediscover placement) remain open.

## Second-person language audit, 2026-09-21, MIGRATION PENDING

Direct follow-on to the card/heading consistency pass below, same live-review session — Roop caught the native Care Chart addressing her in third person ("as they dress," "the mother") instead of speaking to her directly, and asked for a full dig through all mother-facing content.

**Audit method:** a general-purpose subagent searched every mother-facing string across `mobile/`, `src/`, and every content migration for third-person address ("she"/"her"/"the mother"/"they" referring to the mother herself — never flagging "she/her/they" when it correctly refers to the baby, or general third-person statements like "sardines are a lower-mercury choice" that don't address the reader at all). Result: the bug is almost entirely concentrated in one place, not spread randomly — the Nourish postpartum content's templated "Using this shared menu" note (migrations 56/57/58, the toddler-feeding weeks) repeats a third-person version of the same sentence ("Serve the mother an appropriate adult portion... 'if you're still hungry' refers to the mother") across dozens of weeks, plus a couple dozen one-off explanatory sentences drafted the same way in the same content series. Everything else — `CareForYourself.tsx`, Reset, the Mental Health hub, Monthly Chart seed content — was checked and found consistently second-person already.

**Fixed:**
- **`supabase/migration_66_second_person_fixes.sql`** — a PL/pgSQL `DO` block looping over ~21 verified (broken-phrase → fixed-phrase) pairs, applied across every relevant `nourish_week_content` column (`why_it_matters`, `condition_notes`, `meat_fish_eggs_note`, `using_meals_note`, and the per-day `days` jsonb, cast to text for the replace and back) — e.g. "Serve the mother" → "Serve yourself," "refers to the mother" → "refers to you," "for a mother's body after birth" → "for your body after birth." Deliberately targeted phrase-by-phrase, never a blind pronoun swap, so it's safe to rerun (a phrase that's already fixed just stops matching). Fixes whatever is currently live regardless of whether migration 55/56/57 or the later migration_58 rework last wrote a given row — validated via `pglast`, zero parse errors. Also fixes one Care Chart mantra line (postpartum "What You'd Tell Week-One You," from migration_46) that dropped into third person ("She needed... Tell her now") inside an otherwise second-person card — corrected to "You needed... Tell yourself now."
- **Code-level UI copy** (no migration needed): `src/app/page.tsx` (homepage) — "her first 1000 days" → "your first 1000 days," "how her own body is recovering" → "how your own body is recovering," "Her body, mind, and days — cared for too" → "Your body...," "Matched to her pregnancy or postpartum stage" → "Matched to your...". `src/app/dashboard/wealth/page.tsx` and `mobile/app/(tabs)/wealth.tsx` — the "her own security" eyebrow on both platforms → "your own security". `src/app/onboarding/page.tsx` and `src/app/dashboard/confirm-birth/ConfirmBirthClient.tsx` — the delivery-type validation error ("Let us know how she delivered...") → "how you delivered...".

Verified clean on `npx tsc --noEmit` and `npx eslint` (web) and `npx tsc --noEmit` (native) — zero errors across all touched files.

**Status: migration confirmed run in Supabase, changes reviewed live by Roop — "looks good now."** Still needed: push the touched web/native files via GitHub Desktop. This closes item #2 of the 2026-09-21 action list (full second-person audit).

**Roop's one piece of follow-up feedback while reviewing, 2026-09-21:** the "Mental health & support" and "For your care team" cards specifically still need better copy/quotes defined — her words, "which we will do later." **"For your care team" (item #3) is now built — see the section above.** **"Mental health & support" (item #4, PPD/PPA) is now built too — see the "Maternal Mental Health — Phase 2" section above.** Items #6 (Celebrate this week rotation) and #7 (Rediscover placement) remain open.

## Native Care Chart — card/heading consistency pass, 2026-09-21, PUSH PENDING

Roop live-reviewed the native app's daily Care Chart on her phone and flagged it as visually chaotic: Move/Nourish/Reset/Care for yourself/the informational cards (Feeding comfort, If this applies to you, Mental health & support, Celebrate this week, For your care team) were each built on a different card treatment — some plain white cards with a shadow, some plain white with a border, Reset alone tinted with a colored top border — and each module's title used a different font/weight/size. Separately, on the Care for Yourself card specifically, the day/category eyebrow ("MONDAY · FACE TIME") visually outweighed the real heading ("Care for yourself"), the opposite of the intended hierarchy.

Fixed by adding three shared tokens to `mobile/constants/theme.ts` — `moduleCard(accentColor)` (Reset's own tinted-background + colored-top-border shape, now reused by every module so they all share one structure while each keeps its own accent color for quick visual distinction), `moduleTitle` (the one shared bold heading style — Fraunces 700 Bold, indigo, 18px — every module's name now renders in, regardless of module), and `moduleEyebrow` (a small, quiet secondary-caption style for things like the day/category label, deliberately lighter than `moduleTitle` so it can never out-compete the real heading again).

Applied: `ResetOfTheDay.tsx` now actually renders "Reset" as its own bold heading (previously it only ever showed "Need a Reset?" as a small eyebrow with no literal "Reset" heading at all) with "Need a Reset?" demoted to the muted eyebrow above it, and the day's specific activity title moved to a secondary sub-heading. `CareForYourself.tsx`'s header order flipped — "Care for yourself" is now the bold primary heading, the day/category label sits below it as a muted eyebrow — and its card switched from a plain white/bordered box to `moduleCard(Colors.goldDeep)`. `care.tsx`'s Move and Nourish cards, and the generic informational cards (Feeding comfort/Rest support, If this applies to you, Mental health & support, Celebrate this week, For your care team), all switched to a new `dayCard`/`dayCardTitle` pair (`moduleCard(Colors.indigo)` + `moduleTitle`) — kept deliberately separate from the pre-existing `card`/`cardTitle` styles, which the check-in screen (time/energy/mood) still uses and wasn't part of this feedback, so that screen was left untouched.

Verified clean on `npx tsc --noEmit` (zero errors) and `npx expo lint` (18 problems — the exact same pre-existing `react/no-unescaped-entities` baseline already documented elsewhere in this file, zero new issues in any touched file).

**Status: built and verified 2026-09-21, NOT yet pushed via GitHub Desktop.** Roop is reviewing live on her phone via Expo Go before pushing.

## Rediscover — new standalone marketplace module, built web + native, migration confirmed live, 2026-09-19

Direct follow-on to the Care for Yourself work above, same session-arc but a much bigger scope decision. Roop shared an extensive written concept for redefining "Rediscover" — previously a small Care Chart pillar (three tiny reflective Care Steps per phase) — into a real marketplace: "the optional career, talent, passion and collaboration space within Mom's Village." Asked for a critical opinion before anything was built. Through several rounds of clarification (not all decided at once):

- **Pulled entirely out of Care Chart, built as its own standalone module.** Not a tweak, a full architectural move — same "full structural replacement, not a deepening" precedent as Reset and Care for Yourself before it. The old `your_corner` Care Step content is retired (column kept on the DB/type, superseded-comment pattern, same as `care_for_yourself` was before its own rebuild) and replaced everywhere with a pointer card linking into the new module.
- **Community stays exactly what it already is.** Roop's own words, reconfirming her original 2026-08-02 decision: "let the community module be the forum as for discussions and topics in everything only." Community gets one banner link into Rediscover and nothing else — no listings, no needs content, no marketplace UI inside the forum itself.
- **Spotify-style categorized browsing**, not free-text search-first — a mother picks a category family, then a specific tag, both when listing and when browsing.
- **Two-sided listings**: what she offers (Showcase) and what she's looking for (Collaborate / Find Collaborators) are separate content types, not one listing trying to do both.
- **Legal/compliance is Roop's own responsibility** — DPDPA and any other regulatory work is hers to do outside this build.
- **A two-tier disclaimer**: a short version shown right at the point of contact (before messaging someone), and a full standing policy page — "we owe no responsibility, verify on your own."
- **No commission or fee on anything, in V1** — her own words: "we may decide to charge very nominal charge later on... to start with, let's not charge anything." A reasonable next-version option, not built now.
- **Payment stays entirely off-platform** — no integrated payments, just an advisory note to keep screenshots of any payment made or received, "without owing any responsibility to anyone."

**Spec written and reviewed before any code.** Per Roop's ask ("give me a written spec to go through once before we start building"), built as a Claude Docs artifact, then exported to `.docx`/`.pdf` so she could share it with a second AI reviewer (ChatGPT). That review agreed with the core direction and pushed back on three points, all incorporated: (1) don't drop the old "passion discovery" idea entirely — keep a lightweight **Explore** doorway for a mother who doesn't know what she wants yet, rather than requiring her to already know a category; (2) don't build an open 1-5 star rating — the review's own alternative (no rating at all) and Claude's original open-rating idea were both rejected in favor of a synthesis, confirmed with Roop via `AskUserQuestion`: **positive-only "Recommended by N mothers"** — she can recommend once, withdraw it, but there's no mechanism to leave a negative public mark on anyone; (3) confirmed the shop model is **one Rediscover Profile → many Listings**, not one listing per mother.

**Two more open decisions resolved via `AskUserQuestion` before building:**
- **Regulated categories** — legal services, financial/investment advisory, and medical/health advice are **excluded from the category taxonomy entirely at launch**, not just soft-discouraged. Confirmed as a deliberate scope line in `src/lib/rediscoverCategories.ts`'s own header comment — don't add a "Legal," "Financial Advisory," "Investment," or "Medical/Health Advice" tag without Roop's own legal review first.
- **How a mother actually contacts another mother** — the spec didn't originally define this. Flagged to Roop as a real foundational gap rather than silently assuming something lighter (an "I'm interested" tap, or exposing contact info on the profile). She chose the heavier, more complete option: **real in-app private messaging**, one conversation per pair of mothers.

**Schema — `supabase/migration_65_rediscover_schema.sql`.** Eight tables, all RLS-enabled in the same migration that creates them (standing project rule): `user_rediscover_profile` (one row per mother — headline, bio, location, remote_ok, three `open_to_*` flags, is_active); `rediscover_listings` (`category_family` DB-CHECK-constrained to 5 stable values, `category` app-enforced free text — same convention as `user_care_profile.health_flags`, so the tag list can grow without a migration — title, description, is_active); `rediscover_needs` (same category shape, for the Find Collaborators side); `rediscover_recommendations` (composite PK on the recommender/recommended pair, CHECK recommender ≠ recommended — the positive-only trust mechanism); `user_rediscover_saved_listings`; `rediscover_reports` (nullable listing_id/need_id/reported_user_id/message_id + CHECK exactly one is set, insert-only RLS, no select policy — Roop reviews directly in Supabase, same workflow as every other report queue in this project); `rediscover_conversations` (a unique index on `(least(user_a,user_b), greatest(user_a,user_b))` so a pair of mothers can only ever have one conversation, no matter who messages first); `rediscover_messages` (with `read_at`, plus a `bump_rediscover_conversation_last_message()` trigger keeping `last_message_at` in sync, mirroring the project's existing `bump_community_thread_like_count()` pattern). Validated via `pglast` before handoff — 48 statements, zero parse errors.

**Confirmed live in Supabase, 2026-09-19.** Roop ran the migration and flagged that she saw "unsaved data" warnings closing old SQL Editor query tabs afterward and wanted it double-checked. Verified directly via two queries she ran and pasted back: all 8 tables exist in `information_schema.tables`, and every one shows `relrowsecurity = true` in `pg_class` (the `false` rows are just indexes/primary keys, which never carry RLS themselves — expected). The "unsaved data" warning was Supabase's SQL Editor asking about unsaved *draft query text* in a tab, unrelated to whether already-executed statements committed — confirmed this is a non-issue, not a partial migration.

**Category taxonomy — `src/lib/rediscoverCategories.ts` (verbatim ported to `mobile/lib/rediscoverCategories.ts`).** Five families — Products, Creative Services, Professional Services, Business & Operations, Promotion & Creators — each with 7-10 specific tags. A separate `EXPLORE_PROMPTS` array (12 curated entries) powers the Explore doorway: a plain, static "could this be your thing?" list, each entry linking straight into Find pre-filtered to that category. No scoring, no tracking, no pattern-detection engine — that heavier version of "passion discovery" from Roop's original brainstorm stays explicitly deferred.

**Web build — four doors off one landing page (`src/app/dashboard/rediscover/page.tsx`):**
- **Explore** (`explore/page.tsx`) — the curated prompt grid described above.
- **Rediscover Profile** (`profile/page.tsx` + `ProfileForm.tsx`) — headline/bio/location, the three `open_to_*` flags as toggle chips, an `is_active` visibility checkbox, plus a stats strip (her own listing count, need count, and recommend count) linking into Showcase/Collaborate.
- **Showcase** (`showcase/page.tsx` + `ShowcaseManager.tsx`) — full add/edit/hide/delete CRUD over her own listings, family+tag picker, "one listing per product/skill/service, as many as she likes."
- **Find** (`find/page.tsx`) — Spotify-style category browsing (`?family=`/`?category=` query params, both optional), blocked-user filtering, links into listing detail.
- **Listing detail** (`listing/[id]/page.tsx`) — full listing + author card, `RecommendButton`/`SaveButton`/`MessageButton`/`ReportButton`, and `BlockButton` reused directly from `src/app/dashboard/community/[threadId]/BlockButton` (cross-route import, not duplicated) — same `user_blocks` table Community already uses, so a block in one place is a block everywhere.
- **Collaborate** (`collaborate/page.tsx` + `NeedManager.tsx`) — the Find Collaborators feed over `rediscover_needs` (filtered the same way Find filters listings — no AI matching needed, category tags do the work), plus her own needs management below it.
- **Messaging** (`messages/page.tsx` list, `messages/[id]/page.tsx` + `MessageThread.tsx` thread) — real conversations, `read_at` marked on open, `ContactDisclaimer.tsx` (the short tier of the two-tier disclaimer) shown at the top of every thread and on every listing detail page before she can message someone.
- **`src/app/rediscover-disclaimer/page.tsx`** — the full standing policy page (the second tier), built through the existing `LegalPage`/`LegalSection` shell used by every other policy page in this app (Privacy, Terms, Safety, etc.), linked from the dashboard footer and `sitemap.ts`.
- **Community banner** — one gold-accented link card on `src/app/dashboard/community/page.tsx`, with an explanatory code comment quoting Roop's own words so a future session doesn't mistake the boundary for an oversight.

Verified clean on `npx tsc --noEmit` and `npx eslint .` (whole repo) — caught and fixed one real error along the way (`react/no-unescaped-entities` in `NeedManager.tsx`'s "What you've posted" heading).

## Care for Yourself — real 210 Care Notes loaded (replacing placeholders), rotation made type-aware, 2026-09-18

Direct follow-on to the Care for Yourself build in the section immediately below. Roop delivered all 210 real Care Notes in her own voice (30 per category × 7 categories: Hair love/Sunday, Face time/Monday, Hands & feet/Tuesday, Body love/Wednesday, Groom & glow/Thursday, Feel-good Friday/Friday, My care day/Saturday), written to the exact spec preserved below — no weight-loss/bounce-back/fixing-appearance language, procedural/medical topics point toward safe practice and "check with a professional" rather than prescribing treatment, traditional/Ayurvedic content labelled as ritual rather than proven. Alongside the content she gave two explicit process asks, both acted on:

- **"Don't randomly shuffle all 30 cards blindly... have the rotation avoid serving very similar messages consecutively."** Each of the 210 notes was tagged with a `content_type` (KNOW/CARE/RITUAL/FEEL — the original spec's own 4-type taxonomy; her aside also mentioned SAFETY/CONFIDENCE as further options, but the built schema's CHECK constraint and the original spec's own "four styles" section both define exactly four, so tagging stayed within KNOW/CARE/RITUAL/FEEL rather than widening the constraint for a casually-mentioned aside). `pickCareNoteId` in both `src/lib/careForYourselfCalculator.ts` and `mobile/lib/careForYourselfCalculator.ts` now takes `{id, contentType}` pairs instead of bare ids: a new `typeAwareShuffle()` runs the existing seeded Fisher-Yates shuffle, then repairs any position where two adjacent notes (including the wraparound from position 30 back to position 1, since the per-user order is a repeating 30-week cycle) share a `content_type` — swapping the later one for the nearest later note whose type differs from both of its new neighbours, over a few passes since one fix can occasionally create a new collision elsewhere. Fully deterministic for a given seed, same as the shuffle it wraps — no new randomness, no new state to persist. Both call sites (`chart/page.tsx` on web, `care.tsx` on native) updated to pass `{id: n.id, contentType: n.content_type}` instead of bare `n.id`.
- **"I would have the health/skin/hair claims in these 210 cards clinically reviewed before production, especially pregnancy/breastfeeding, hair loss, chemical treatments and traditional/Ayurvedic content."** Flagging this back rather than silently proceeding, per this project's standing verify-before-lock discipline (Nourish, Move, every other content series): the 210 notes were written throughout with real hedging already built in — "check with a healthcare professional," "deserves appropriate professional assessment," "check uncertain actives or treatments with an appropriate clinician," Ayurvedic/traditional practices explicitly labelled as ritual/enjoyment rather than proven claims — and every concrete factual claim in them (postpartum shedding being common, sun protection reducing photoageing, frequent handwashing/sanitiser drying skin, persistent nail/skin changes warranting assessment, stronger chemical peels needing professional aftercare) matches well-established, non-controversial dermatology/hair-care knowledge, not a new or contested claim. Given that, this pass did **not** run a fresh WebSearch verification per claim the way Nourish's numeric RDA figures needed — but Roop's own request for a real dermatology/trichology review before calling this content clinically settled is a genuine, valid next step, not yet done, and is recorded here so it isn't lost. **Loaded with `safety_flag = 'draft'`** (not `'approved'`) specifically to reflect this — real content, written with care, but not yet signed off by a qualified reviewer the way she asked.

**Schema/content — `supabase/migration_63_care_for_yourself_real_content.sql`.** Deletes any `safety_flag = 'placeholder'` rows left over from `migration_62` (making `migration_62` itself skippable — Roop was told to run only `migration_61` then `migration_63`), then inserts all 210 real notes via the same `on conflict (id) do update` idempotent pattern as every content migration in this project. `migration_62`'s 7 placeholder rows (including Roop's own Monday/Face example, used verbatim in her spec) are now fully superseded — her real Monday/Face note (`CARE-FACE-001`, "Consistency Beats the Shelf") replaces it.

**Status: migrations 61 and 63 confirmed run in Supabase by Roop, 2026-09-18. Rotation-logic update verified clean on `npx tsc --noEmit` and `npx eslint .` (web) and `npx tsc --noEmit` (native).** Still needed: (1) push all touched files via GitHub Desktop — `src/lib/careForYourselfCalculator.ts`, `mobile/lib/careForYourselfCalculator.ts`, `src/app/dashboard/care/chart/page.tsx`, `mobile/app/(tabs)/care.tsx`, plus `supabase/migration_63_care_for_yourself_real_content.sql` for the repo record — and let Vercel redeploy; (2) sync native to her phone (`npx expo start`, reload in Expo Go); (3) live-check that each day of the week now shows a real note (not a placeholder) on both web and phone.

## Care for Yourself — Roop's explicit call: no outside clinical review, general disclaimer instead, content finalized/approved, 2026-09-18

Direct follow-on to the section immediately above. Right after the real 210 notes were loaded with `safety_flag = 'draft'` pending the dermatology/trichology review she'd asked for, Roop closed that loop herself — the other way. Her own words, preserved verbatim since this is the actual governing decision: **"Never wanted it to be reviewed by anyone. Those are the general statements open for them to follow or not follow. We can always have a disclaimer that... before trying anything, they need to go by their [own judgment] because their skin might be allergic to any... any sort of disclaimer that will waive us off. These are more or less general statements which are not affecting too much. So I want to go ahead with this. Don't draft it. Just finalize it. Consider them approved."**

Acted on directly, not treated as something to draft/propose first, per her explicit "don't draft it, just finalize it":

- **`supabase/migration_64_care_for_yourself_approved.sql`** — `update care_for_yourself_notes set safety_flag = 'approved', last_reviewed_date = current_date where safety_flag = 'draft';`. No schema change needed — `'approved'` was already a valid value under migration_61's own CHECK constraint, this just moves all 210 real rows into it. **Not yet run in Supabase** — same "paste into the SQL editor" pattern as every migration in this project; needs to be run alongside the GitHub Desktop push still pending from the section above.
- **A general liability-waiving disclaimer, rendered on every Care for Yourself card** (`src/components/CareForYourself.tsx` and `mobile/components/CareForYourself.tsx`), shown right below the note/tiny-action and above the "♡ Made time for me" button, every time: *"General suggestions, not tailored medical advice — patch test anything new, and check with a healthcare professional if you have allergies, a skin or hair condition, or are pregnant or breastfeeding."* This is the disclaimer mechanism Roop asked for in place of a per-note professional review — small, muted caption text (matches this app's existing disclaimer styling elsewhere), shown unconditionally rather than gated to any specific note.
- Both components' header comments updated to stop describing the content as awaiting review/placeholder and instead record this decision plainly, so a future session reading the code doesn't reintroduce the "needs clinical sign-off" framing this section explicitly closes out.

**Why this is a reasonable place to land, not just deferring to Roop's preference:** every concrete factual claim across the 210 notes was already checked, at drafting time, against well-established, non-controversial dermatology/hair-care knowledge (see the section above), and the notes were written throughout with real hedging built in — "check with a healthcare professional," Ayurvedic/traditional practices explicitly labelled as ritual rather than proven. What's added now is the standard general-audience safety net (patch-test/allergy/pregnancy-breastfeeding caution) that this app already uses elsewhere for comparable general-guidance content, rather than an individualized-advice review process that Roop has decided the actual risk level of this content doesn't call for.
