## Care for Yourself — new seven-day personal-care rhythm module, built 2026-09-18, NOT YET DEPLOYED

Direct follow-on to the Care Chart card-consistency pass above, same session. Roop: "So now the reset is done, and I have developed a strategy for care for yourself module as well" — a full structural replacement for the existing generic "Care for yourself" pillar, same "full structural replacement, not a tweak" precedent as the Reset-of-the-day rebuild. She delivered a complete written spec (reproduced in full below, verbatim) and answered 4 clarifying questions via AskUserQuestion before any code was written — all four are now confirmed, binding decisions:

- **Content authorship: she writes all 210 real Care Notes herself — Claude only builds the plumbing.** Unlike Reset (where Claude drafted an initial ~30-activity list for her to rework), Claude does NOT write the real content this time. This build ships 7 clearly-flagged placeholder notes (`safety_flag = 'placeholder'`, one per category, including her own Monday/Face example verbatim) purely so the feature is demonstrable — replacing them with her real 210 is her own next step, not Claude's.
- **Replace the old pillar everywhere.** The new module fully replaces the old generic per-week `care_chart_week_content.care_for_yourself` text field across all 197 already-built weeks (pregnancy 1-39 + postpartum 0-156), wherever a mother actually sees her real chart — same precedent as Reset replacing the old mood-mapped Reset card. The old field is left on the DB/type (column still exists, still selected) but marked superseded in code comments, same pattern as `ResetContent`'s own superseded-but-kept type.
- **Saturday ("My care day") gets its own dedicated 30-note bank** (`CARE-MYCARE-001` → `030`), not a "revisit another category" mechanic — a genuine seventh content bank, same shape as the other six.
- **Built for web and native together, in this same session** — not web-first-then-native-later (which is how Reset was originally sequenced).

**The full spec, Roop's own words, preserved here as the source of truth for all 210 real notes she'll eventually write:**

> **CARE FOR YOURSELF — Core Purpose.** Care for Yourself is a gentle seven-day personal-care rhythm inside the mother's Care Chart. Its purpose is not to change, correct or "restore" a mother's appearance. There should be no weight-loss language, bounce-back messaging, body comparison or implication that she needs fixing. The underlying philosophy is: She is already enough exactly as she is. Care is not correction — it is attention. Mom's Village simply makes sure that while she is caring for everyone else, she continues making a little space for her own face, hair, body, grooming and sense of self.
>
> **1. The seven-day care rhythm** — each day of the week is permanently assigned one area of personal care: Sunday — 💆‍♀️ Hair love (hair, scalp, conditioning, washing, oiling traditions, styling, breakage prevention, heat protection, postpartum hair awareness, general hair-care habits). Monday — ✨ Face time (general facial care, hydration, sun protection, healthy ageing, gentle skincare principles, facial massage, skin-barrier awareness, neck/décolletage care, safe skincare habits — must NOT prescribe a specific skincare routine, since different skin types/conditions need different care). Tuesday — 💅 Hands & feet (hands, feet, nails, cuticles, heels, moisturising, manicure/pedicure care, footwear-related care, traditional rituals, safe salon/home-care practices). Wednesday — 🧴 Body love (whole-body care: moisturising, bathing, body oils, gentle exfoliation principles, fragrance, stretch-mark/body-change acceptance, comfort, general body-care rituals). Thursday — 🌸 Groom & glow (personal grooming per her own preferences: brows, facial/body hair, shaving, waxing, threading, oral freshness, salon hygiene, small grooming rituals — always a choice, never an expectation). Friday — 💄 Feel-good Friday (confidence and presentation rather than treatment: clothes, jewellery, perfume, makeup, hair, colour, personal style, posture — "what makes me feel good when I see myself," not about looking thinner/younger/more conventionally attractive). Saturday — 🌷 My care day (flexible — she can choose whichever part of herself she feels like caring for, revisit something from the week, take a longer ritual, book a salon appointment, or simply rest; never a "catch-up day" for care she failed to complete).
>
> **2. How each daily card works** — the Care Chart automatically surfaces the right card by day of week, no questionnaire before opening it. Her own example: "✨ MONDAY / FACE TIME / Today's Care Note / 'Your skincare doesn't have to be complicated to be worthwhile. A few products that genuinely suit your skin and that you use consistently can be more useful than constantly chasing the newest routine.' / Today: Make a few unrushed minutes for the facial care that already works for you. / ♡ Made time for me."
>
> **3. Rotating Care Notes** — each of the 7 categories should initially contain 30 approved notes (210 total for V1). Every time that day/category occurs, a different unused note shows; after all 30 have been shown, the cycle restarts/shuffles unless more content has been added; avoid showing the same note on consecutive occurrences.
>
> **4. Content mix** — the 30 notes per category should be internally balanced across roughly four styles: KNOW (short, useful, evidence-based information), CARE (a practical, broadly applicable reminder not dependent on a particular skin/hair type), RITUAL (cultural/traditional/Ayurvedic practices, clearly labelled as traditional rather than presented as scientifically proven where evidence is uncertain), FEEL (confidence, humour, permission, self-worth, positive relationship with her current appearance/body). These labels are for content organisation only, not necessarily user-facing.
>
> **5. Content data structure** — each note gets its own content ID (e.g. `CARE-HAIR-001`→`030`, `CARE-FACE-001`→`030`, `CARE-HANDSFEET-001`→`030`, `CARE-BODY-001`→`030`, `CARE-GROOM-001`→`030`, `CARE-FEELGOOD-001`→`030`, `CARE-MYCARE-001`→`030`), with: ID, Day, Category, Content type (KNOW/CARE/RITUAL/FEEL), Headline, Care Note, optional tiny action, safety flag/review status, last reviewed date — so the library can grow later without changing the architecture.
>
> **6. Content safety** — never diagnose skin/hair/body conditions through these cards; avoid specific treatment recommendations needing individual assessment; anything touching chemical peels/retinoids/strong acids/pregnancy-breastfeeding considerations/significant hair loss/infections/persistent problems/medical conditions should focus on general education, safety and when professional guidance is appropriate, not prescribe treatment; Ayurvedic/traditional practices clearly distinguished from evidence-based medical advice.
>
> **7. Completion mechanism** — one soft action, ♡ Made time for me. No streaks, no missed-day warnings, no overdue tasks, no "2/7 completed," no red crosses, no failure states, no guilt-based notifications. At week's end the app may simply acknowledge "You made some space for yourself this week. ♡" regardless of how many cards were completed.
>
> **8. Difference from Reset** — the two must stay behaviourally distinct. Reset: spontaneous, unpredictable, mood-lifting, "give me something to do right now." Care for Yourself: rhythmic, predictable category, fresh rotating knowledge/inspiration, "remind me not to disappear from my own care." Reset changes her moment; Care for Yourself protects her habit of caring for herself. Experience principle: she should know what KIND of care Monday brings, but never know in advance what Mom's Village will tell her that particular Monday. Predictable ritual, fresh content, zero pressure.

**Schema — `supabase/migration_61_care_for_yourself_schema.sql`.** Two tables, not three — a planned `user_care_note_seen` "seen history" table turned out to be unnecessary once the rotation logic was actually designed (see below), so it was dropped before writing any migration, keeping the schema simpler than first sketched. `care_for_yourself_notes` (the content bank — `id` text primary key in the `CARE-<CATEGORY>-NNN` format from her spec, `category` check-constrained to the 7 keys, `note_number`, `content_type` check-constrained to KNOW/CARE/RITUAL/FEEL, `headline`, `care_note`, optional `tiny_action`, `safety_flag` defaulting to `'placeholder'`, `last_reviewed_date`, `is_active`) — read-only for authenticated users, no write policy at all, authored only via migration, same convention as `reset_activities`/`monthly_chart_content`. `user_care_completions` (`user_id`, `completed_date`, `category`, optional `note_id`, composite primary key on user+date+category) — insert/update-as-self only, the only place any "done" state lives, and it only ever supports a gentle weekly count, never a target or score.

**Rotation logic — `src/lib/careForYourselfCalculator.ts` / `mobile/lib/careForYourselfCalculator.ts` (verbatim ports, same "calculator" pattern as `resetCalculator.ts`/`weekCalculator.ts`).** `careCategoryForDate(dateIso)` maps a calendar date to one of the 7 fixed categories via `getUTCDay()` (parsed manually via `Date.UTC` to avoid timezone-shift bugs near midnight, same precedent as every other date-sensitive calculator in this project). `pickCareNoteId(userId, category, activeNoteIds, dateIso)` is the actual rotation-without-repeat mechanism, and deliberately needs no database "seen" history at all: each mother gets her own fixed pseudo-random ordering of that category's notes (seeded by a hash of her own user id + the category, via a small FNV-1a hash + mulberry32 seeded PRNG + Fisher-Yates shuffle — so two mothers see different sequences of the same content), and which position in that fixed ordering is "today" is simply the number of whole weeks since a fixed epoch (2020-01-01). Since every category maps to exactly one weekday, that count increments by exactly 1 every time that weekday comes around — so consecutive occurrences always land on distinct, adjacent positions, and the whole bank must complete one full lap before any note can repeat, which already satisfies the spec's "different unused note every occurrence, cycle restarts once exhausted, no back-to-back repeats" requirement with zero extra state to write. It also means the bank can grow later (more notes added to a category) without any schema or logic change — a longer array just makes the lap longer. `careWeekAcknowledgment(count)` returns the gentle weekly line, or `null` when count is 0 (per the spec's explicit "no message at all if nothing was completed," never a "0/7").

**Content — `supabase/migration_62_care_for_yourself_seed.sql`.** 7 placeholder rows, one per category, idempotent (`on conflict (id) do update`, same pattern as every content seed in this project). The Monday/Face row is not invented — it's Roop's own example text from her spec message, split into headline/note/tiny-action verbatim. The other 6 are plainly-labelled "Placeholder — [category]" filler, explicitly there so the feature renders and is testable, not as draft real content. **These 7 rows are meant to be deleted and replaced once Roop writes the real 210** — `delete from care_for_yourself_notes where safety_flag = 'placeholder';` is the intended cleanup step, documented in the migration's own header comment.

**Web — `src/components/CareForYourself.tsx`.** Renders through the shared `PillarCard` (built in the card-consistency pass above), collapsed by default, its header eyebrow showing the day+category (e.g. "✨ MONDAY · FACE TIME"), title "Care for yourself," and — inside the body — the note's headline, the care note text, an italic "Today: ..." tiny action if present, a single "♡ Made time for me" button (upserts into `user_care_completions`, flips to "♥ Made time for me" once done, disabled once clicked), and the gentle weekly acknowledgment line (optimistically counting today's just-clicked completion, never shown if the count is 0). `PillarCard`'s own `done` prop gives the title a soft strikethrough once she's made time for herself that day, matching every other card's visual language. Wired into `src/components/CareWeekContent.tsx`: the old `{hasContent(week.care_for_yourself) && <WeekCard>...}` block is gone, replaced with `<CareForYourself category={careForYourselfCategory} note={careForYourselfNote} .../>` in the exact same position in the linear card list (right after Rest support, before Your corner). `src/app/dashboard/care/chart/page.tsx` fetches the day's category, the active notes for that category, picks today's note via `pickCareNoteId`, and computes the trailing-7-day completion count/today's-done state, passing all four into `CareWeekContent`.

**Native — `mobile/components/CareForYourself.tsx`.** Same content/behavior, built as its own self-contained collapsible card (native has no shared `PillarCard`-equivalent component yet — every screen inlines its own `ExpandableCard`-style pattern, so this follows that same established convention: a `Pressable` header with an icon badge + chevron, an `open` state, the note/action/acknowledgment in the expanded body). Wired into `mobile/app/(tabs)/care.tsx`: the old `{hasContent(week.care_for_yourself) && <ExpandableCard icon="hand-left-outline" .../>}` block is gone, replaced with `<CareForYourself .../>` in the same position; `load()` gained the same category/note/completion-count fetching as the web page, using the identical calculator functions ported verbatim.

**Deliberately NOT replaced: the old phase-based (`weekly_care_chart_content`) preview path.** `chart/page.tsx`'s legacy `SECTIONS`/`bySection` grid (reachable only via Roop's own manual `?phase=` review links, never by a real subscriber — every real journey week 1-196 already resolves through the new `care_chart_week_content` system) still shows the old "skin"/"Care for yourself" section from the deprecated `weekly_care_chart_content` table. This is intentionally untouched — it's dead code for real mothers now (same as the "mind"/Reset section in that same legacy grid, which the Reset rebuild didn't touch either), and wiring the new module into a reviewer-only preview path wasn't worth the added complexity. Flagging here so a future session doesn't mistake this for an oversight.

Verified clean on `npx tsc --noEmit` and `npx eslint`/`npx expo lint` on both platforms — web: zero errors across all touched/new files. Native: `expo lint` shows the same standing 18-problem baseline (`react/no-unescaped-entities` on pre-existing lines in `care.tsx`, `index.tsx`, `library-reader.tsx`, `vaccinations.tsx`/`vaccinations-log.tsx`, `wealth-savings.tsx`, `wealth-schemes.tsx`) already documented elsewhere in this file — confirmed the two hits now inside `care.tsx` are the same pre-existing "This week's content isn't loaded yet" line that simply shifted down in the file, not anything introduced this pass; zero new issues anywhere.

**Status: fully built and verified, NOT yet deployed.** Still needed from Roop: (1) run `migration_61_care_for_yourself_schema.sql` then `migration_62_care_for_yourself_seed.sql` in Supabase's SQL editor, in that order; (2) push all touched/new web and native files via GitHub Desktop and let Vercel redeploy; (3) sync the native change to her phone (`npx expo start`, reload in Expo Go); (4) live-check that each day of the week shows the right category and a real (currently placeholder) note, on both web and her phone; (5) — her own task, not Claude's — write the real 210 Care Notes (30 per category × 7 categories) following the spec above, then have Claude swap them in and delete the 7 placeholder rows. "Care for yourself" is now the 4th of the 5 Care Chart pillars to get a full structural rebuild (after Reset, and alongside the card-consistency pass) — only Rediscover remains on its original, simpler per-week text-field design.

## Care Chart — web cards unified into one collapsible pattern (font/layout consistency pass) — built 2026-09-18, PUSHED — awaiting Vercel redeploy + live check

Roop live-reviewed the deployed Care Chart on web (screenshots of Move, Nourish, Care for yourself, and the new Reset-of-the-day card) and gave two pieces of feedback: (1) "the font is different for all the modules... there needs to be uniformity in headers... font type and size wise," and (2) "this still looks little chaotic to me... can we make all tabs linear... where user clicks to expand?" Confirmed two scope questions via AskUserQuestion before touching code: each section expands/collapses **independently** (not a strict one-open-at-a-time accordion — matches how the native app's `ExpandableCard`s already behave), and this pass is scoped to the **Care Chart page only** (Monthly Chart's six category cards are untouched).

**Root cause of the "different font" complaint.** Every header was already rendering in Fraunces (`.font-display`, weight 500, per `globals.css`) — it was never actually a different *font family*. The real inconsistency was in size/hierarchy: `MoveSection`'s own header used `text-xl` (20px), the new `ResetOfTheDay`'s used `text-lg` (18px), the generic `WeekCard` title (Nourish/Hydration goal/Care for yourself/etc.) used `text-base` (16px), and — the one that actually looked like two different fonts stacked — `NourishMealCard` rendered its own second, separate header (`nourishWeek.theme_title`, `text-[15px]`) *nested inside* a generic `WeekCard` that already had its own plain "Nourish" title above it. So Nourish visually had two headers of two different sizes on top of each other, while Care for yourself had only one, plain one — reading as "every module uses a different font."

**Fix — one shared component, `src/components/PillarCard.tsx`.** Every section on the Care Chart (Move, Reset, Nourish, Hydration goal, Feeding comfort, Rest support, Care for yourself, Your corner, Support moment, Celebrate this week, Mental health & support) now renders through this single component, so the header typography is identical everywhere: an optional small-caps eyebrow (11px, accent-colored), one `text-lg` Fraunces title (indigo), an optional italic mantra/quote line (`text-[14px]`, sage-deep) — exactly one spec, not four. `PillarCard` also owns the collapse/expand behavior itself: collapsed by default (`defaultOpen=false` everywhere), each card's own `useState` so opening one never affects any other. Deliberately **not** a single `<button>` wrapping the whole header row — the optional "done" checkmark needs its own real `<button>`, and a button nested inside another button is invalid HTML and breaks screen readers, so the title area and the collapse chevron are each their own sibling button instead.

**Refactor, not a rewrite — every card's actual content and behavior is unchanged:**
- `WeekCard` (used for Nourish-without-meal-data/Hydration goal/Feeding comfort/Rest support/Care for yourself/Your corner/Support moment/Celebrate this week/Mental health & support) now renders through `PillarCard`, keeping its existing per-card "mark done" upsert/delete into `user_care_week_progress` exactly as before.
- `MoveSection`'s big hero header (theme + mantra) now flows into `PillarCard`'s `eyebrow`/`title`/`subtitle` props instead of its own bespoke header markup; its whole rich body (tiers3/sections branching, featured exercise, quote, "why this helps," collapsed safety note, recovery pills, closing/milestone content) is unchanged, just moved to be `PillarCard`'s children.
- `ResetOfTheDay.tsx` (the 2026-09-16 Reset-of-the-day rebuild) now renders through `PillarCard` too (`eyebrow="Need a Reset?"`, `icon={activity.emoji}`, `title={activity.title}`) — the offer/skipped/done phase machine, the badge unlock, and the Share-to-Gallery link are all unchanged, just moved inside the collapsible body. The "Reset done ✓" line moved from the header into the body itself.
- `NourishMealCard` no longer nests inside a generic `WeekCard` — it now calls `PillarCard` directly, with its real eyebrow (`Nourish · Week N postpartum · Day D — <today's title>`), its real title (`nourishWeek.theme_title`), and its real mantra as the subtitle — removing the double-header entirely. Its meal-slot grid, protein tip, and "more about this week" disclosure are unchanged.
- A small shared `useCardDoneToggle(weekNumber, cardKey, initiallyDone)` hook and a shared `<DoneButton>` component were pulled out of `WeekCard`/`MoveSection`'s previously-duplicated (twice, now three times before this refactor) Supabase upsert/delete logic — one implementation, not three copies that could quietly drift apart.
- Layout: the smaller pillar cards' section changed from `grid md:grid-cols-2 gap-4` to a single-column `space-y-3` list — a 2-column grid doesn't reflow cleanly once cards expand independently to different heights, and a straight top-to-bottom list is what actually delivers "linear, click to expand." Move and Reset now sit inside the same single `space-y-3` list as everything else (previously they were separate full-width blocks above a 2-column grid) — the whole Care Chart body, once the top summary header renders, is now one consistent vertical list of collapsed cards.

Verified clean on `npx tsc --noEmit` and `npx eslint` against all three touched/new files (`PillarCard.tsx`, `CareWeekContent.tsx`, `ResetOfTheDay.tsx`) — zero errors, zero new warnings.

**Scope, deliberately not touched this pass:** the native app already has its own `ExpandableCard`/`CollapsibleCard` collapse pattern for Care (built during the Phase 9 visual-polish pass) — not modified here, not audited for the same header-size consistency, since Roop was reviewing the website specifically. Monthly Chart's six category cards on the dashboard home are untouched (explicit scope decision, confirmed via AskUserQuestion). Worth a look if she likes this pattern and wants it carried elsewhere.

**Status: built, verified, and pushed via GitHub Desktop, 2026-09-18.** Vercel should auto-deploy from this push. Still needed: live-check on momvillage.in that every Care Chart section now opens/closes independently with a consistent header, on both a Move-heavy week and a week with the Nourish meal card.

## Reset rebuilt from a mood-mapped card into a full "Reset of the day" feature — built 2026-09-14/16, NOT YET DEPLOYED

Roop's instruction: rebuild the Care Chart's Reset heading to be "as dynamic as our Move chart and the Nourish chart" — a full structural replacement, not a tweak. This session covered Reset only; "Care for yourself" and the 5th module (Rediscover) are explicitly deferred to future sessions using the same approach. Full spec arrived as a mix of dictated product vision and several rounds of clarifying questions (AskUserQuestion used three times: media types/replace-vs-add/authorship; Gallery interaction level; consistency-reward mechanism) — every real decision below was confirmed with her directly, not inferred.

**What replaced the old system.** The old Reset card was one of several `WeekCard`s in a 2-column grid, its content picked purely by that day's mood score (`RESET_KEY_BY_MOOD`, mapping mood_score 1-5 to a fixed message per phase/week). That's gone entirely — removed from `CareWeekContent.tsx`'s grid and from `care.tsx`'s native equivalent, along with the `RESET_KEY_BY_MOOD` constant on both platforms. `HeavyDaySafetyBridge` (the gentle "is anything worrying you" PPD-adjacent check) now triggers off `moodScore <= 1` directly instead of off `resetKey === "heavy_day"`, since the mood-keyed Reset variant it used to piggyback on no longer exists.

**New flow, confirmed and locked with Roop:**
- One fixed **"Reset of the day"** — the same card for the entire village on a given calendar date, not per-mood, not reshuffled if she doesn't like it. Roop explicitly asked to drop alternate-suggestion/reshuffle entirely ("let's skip this idea where we are giving her alternate research if she doesn't like the current one").
- A no-guilt **"not today"** skip — never recorded anywhere, no streak broken, nothing to feel bad about.
- **"I'm doing this"** → Reset Done, recorded in `user_reset_completions` (one row per user per day, unique constraint, `on conflict` treated as a no-op success rather than an error).
- Optional **"Share your Reset with the Village"** — text, photo, video (≤60s), or audio (≤60s) — landing in a **Reset Gallery inside Community**, explicitly for browsing/inspiration, not relationship-building ("Community remains the place to actually connect"). An "artistic encouragement" nudge line appears in the share composer, per Roop's request to invite creativity/aesthetics rather than a flat submission.
- Gallery interaction is heart-tap only — no comments, no visible "liked by" list (Roop's confirmed "Option B"). Sort is always chronological, **never** algorithmic/ranked-by-hearts — she was explicit hearts must not become a de facto ranking algorithm.
- Gallery scrolling is **deliberately unbounded**, a genuine departure from this app's own standing "no endless feed" rule everywhere else. Roop's reasoning, worth preserving verbatim since it's the actual design principle: "I don't want to give her endless scrolling when she's making the decision... If she wants to just see the resetting activities of other mothers, then the scrolling is her choice, not the overwhelming task we are giving her... If she doesn't want to see, she can skip." The distinction that resolved this against the app's own anti-feed principle: engineered compulsion (algorithmic/auto-loading feeds designed to maximize engagement) is what's banned; voluntary, self-paced browsing she opts into is not the same thing. Implemented as manual "Show more" pagination (`PAGE_SIZE = 30`, `?limit=`/`limit` state) — a real content increase on tap, never an auto-loading scroll trigger.
- **Cumulative, never-resetting milestone badges** instead of a true streak. Roop's original ask was a real streak-with-badge-and-no-penalty-on-break; Claude pushed back on the streak mechanic specifically, citing this project's own repeatedly-documented "no streak/score, non-punitive" design principle (Care Chart check-ins, Mental Health/PPD hub, prior CLAUDE.md entries) and the psychological "I lost it" feeling a broken streak carries even with no visible penalty. Offered cumulative milestone badges as a concrete alternative via AskUserQuestion; Roop chose it as the recommended option. `RESET_BADGE_THRESHOLDS`/`RESET_BADGE_LABELS`/`unlockedResetBadge`/`nextResetBadge`/`justUnlockedResetBadge` are pure counters off `user_reset_completions`'s total row count — they only go up, never reset.
- Village-wide daily rotation is **deterministic, not random**: `pickDailyResetIndex(activityCount, todayIso)` = `daysSinceEpoch(todayIso) % activityCount`, so every mother sees the same card on the same calendar date. This was a judgment call, not an explicit instruction — flagged as such — made because it's what makes the Gallery fun (many mothers doing the "same" Reset differently, visible side by side).

**Content — 30 locked cards, Roop's own final voice, not Claude's draft.** Claude drafted an initial ~30-activity list per her ask; she took it away, reworked it herself end-to-end, and delivered the final 30 (title/emoji/body) directly as chat text after a first attempted-but-missing file attachment. Her own framing, worth preserving: "This is the voice I'd use for Reset across Mom's Village: slightly cheeky, warm, spontaneous and sometimes rebellious... And some Resets should be deliberately silly. That's important. Motherhood content is already full of breathing, gratitude, journaling and 'remember to hydrate.' Reset can be the corner of Mom's Village where she's allowed to have a little fun." All 30 are seeded verbatim, exactly as she wrote them.

**Schema — `supabase/migration_59_reset_feature_schema.sql`:** `reset_activities` (30 seeded rows, read-only-for-authenticated RLS, same pattern as `monthly_chart_content`/`weekly_care_chart_content`); `user_reset_completions` (insert-as-self, one row per user per day, unique constraint); `reset_shares` (insert-as-self/delete-own-row; a CHECK constraint requires text-type shares to have a `caption` and no `media_path`, and non-text types to have `media_path`); `reset_share_hearts` + a `bump_reset_share_heart_count()` trigger mirroring the existing `bump_community_thread_like_count()` pattern; `community_reports` extended with a nullable `reset_share_id` column and a widened 3-way CHECK, reusing Community's existing `is_hidden` soft-moderation convention rather than building anything new. Validated via `pglast` (28 statements, zero parse errors) before handoff.

**Storage — a deliberate departure from this project's own convention.** Every existing bucket (`vaccination-cards`, `voice-logs`, `memory-photos`) is private, per-user-folder, signed-URL-only. The new `reset-shares` bucket is marked `public: true` in `storage.buckets` on purpose — the whole point of the Gallery is other mothers browsing shared media on one screen, so serving directly via `getPublicUrl()` avoids needing a signed URL per viewer per item. Upload/delete RLS is still restricted to the uploader's own folder; only read access is public.

**Content seed — `supabase/migration_60_reset_activities_seed.sql`:** 30 per-row `insert ... on conflict (card_number) do update` statements (idempotent, safe to rerun — same pattern as every content migration in this project), dollar-quoted to avoid manual apostrophe-escaping. Validated via `pglast` (30 statements, zero errors).

**Web build:**
- `src/lib/resetCalculator.ts` — `pickDailyResetIndex`, badge logic (described above).
- `src/components/ResetOfTheDay.tsx` — the card itself: `"offer" | "skipped" | "done"` phase state machine, `markDone()` handling the `23505` unique-violation as a non-error, badge display, Share CTA to `/dashboard/community/reset-share/new?activityId=`.
- `src/app/dashboard/community/reset-share/new/page.tsx` + `ResetShareForm.tsx` — subscription-gated composer, 4-tab media picker (text/photo/video/audio), client-side 60s duration checks (hidden `<video>` probe for video; `MediaRecorder` + a hard `setTimeout(stopRecording, 60000)` auto-stop for audio, rather than relying on her to watch a timer), uploads to `reset-shares`, inserts into `reset_shares`.
- `src/app/dashboard/community/reset-gallery/page.tsx` + `HeartButton.tsx` — the Gallery: `PAGE_SIZE = 30`, `?limit=` manual pagination, `user_blocks` filtering, author names via `community_author_names`, per-viewer hearted state, referenced-activity display, media-type-specific rendering.
- `CareWeekContent.tsx` — `ResetOfTheDay` slotted in as its own full-width block after Move (not in the 2-column grid); old `WeekCard`/`RESET_KEY_BY_MOOD` Reset removed.
- `chart/page.tsx` — computes `resetActivityRow` via the daily-rotation index, fetches total + today's completions, passes all three into `CareWeekContent`.
- `community/[threadId]/ReportButton.tsx` — optional `resetShareId` prop threaded into the report insert.
- `community/page.tsx` — a Reset Gallery banner ("✨ See what other mothers are resetting with today") before the search bar.

Verified clean on `npx tsc --noEmit` and `npx eslint` across all 10 touched/created web files.

**Native build — mirrors the web build file-for-file, same conventions as every other native port in this project (copy-paste-safe shared logic, manual sync, no shared package):**
- `mobile/lib/resetCalculator.ts` — verbatim port of the web version.
- `mobile/components/ResetOfTheDay.tsx` — RN port using `View`/`Text`/`Pressable`/`ActivityIndicator`, `router.push` for the share CTA.
- `mobile/components/ResetHeartButton.tsx` — native heart toggle, same logic as web's `HeartButton.tsx`.
- `mobile/app/reset-share-new.tsx` — full native share composer: `ImagePicker.launchImageLibraryAsync` for photo/video (`videoMaxDuration: MAX_MEDIA_SECONDS` plus a manual `asset.duration` check), `Audio.Recording.createAsync` for audio with the same hard 60s `setTimeout` auto-stop as web, all three media types uploaded via the established `FileSystem.readAsStringAsync` (from `expo-file-system/legacy`) → `decodeBase64` → `supabase.storage.upload()` pattern already used in `memories.tsx`. Uses `DrillHeader`.
- `mobile/app/reset-gallery.tsx` — `ScrollView` (not `FlatList`) rendering cards, `expo-av`'s `Video`/`Audio`/`ResizeMode` for playback (tap-to-play for voice notes via `Audio.Sound.createAsync`), `limit` state + "Show more" Pressable for the same manual, non-auto-loading pagination as web. Uses `DrillHeader` (corrected from an initial incorrect `ScreenHeader` import — this is a pushed screen, not a tab root).
- `mobile/app/(tabs)/care.tsx` — fetches `reset_activities`/completions the same way `chart/page.tsx` does on web, passes into a conditional `<ResetOfTheDay />` replacing the old `<ExpandableCard title="Reset" summary={week.reset[resetKey]} />`; `RESET_KEY_BY_MOOD` removed here too.
- `mobile/app/(tabs)/community.tsx` — a matching Reset Gallery banner Pressable before "Start a discussion."

**Native verification.** `npx tsc --noEmit` initially surfaced 3 errors, all one class: `router.push("/reset-gallery")` (in `community.tsx` and `reset-share-new.tsx`) and the templated `router.push(\`/reset-share-new?activityId=...\`)` (in `ResetOfTheDay.tsx`) were flagged because the two new route strings weren't yet present in Expo Router's auto-generated, **gitignored** typed-routes file (`mobile/.expo/types/router.d.ts`) — that file only regenerates when `npx expo start` boots Metro, and this sandbox's network proxy blocks the dependency-version-check call `expo start` makes before Metro ever runs, so it couldn't be regenerated here. Confirmed this is the sandbox-only cache-staleness class of issue (same category as the Google-Fonts-unreachable and `api.razorpay.com`-blocked notes already logged elsewhere in this file) by manually patching the three affected union-type lines in the local `.expo/types/router.d.ts` (purely a local verification aid — this file is never committed and Roop's own `expo start` will regenerate it correctly the first time she runs the app) — `npx tsc --noEmit` then came back **fully clean**, confirming no other real type errors exist anywhere in the native Reset code. `npx expo lint` shows 18 problems, all `react/no-unescaped-entities` on pre-existing baseline lines in files this session touched for unrelated reasons (`care.tsx`, `index.tsx`, `library-reader.tsx`, `vaccinations.tsx`/`vaccinations-log.tsx`, `wealth-savings.tsx`, `wealth-schemes.tsx`) — spot-checked several of the flagged lines directly and confirmed they predate this session's edits, and zero new Reset files (`reset-share-new.tsx`, `reset-gallery.tsx`, `ResetOfTheDay.tsx`, `ResetHeartButton.tsx`) appear anywhere in the lint output.

**Status: fully built and verified, NOT yet deployed.** Still needed from Roop: (1) run `migration_59_reset_feature_schema.sql` then `migration_60_reset_activities_seed.sql` in Supabase's SQL editor, in that order; (2) push all touched/new web and native files via GitHub Desktop; (3) sync the native change to her phone (`npx expo start`, reload in Expo Go — this is also the step that will regenerate the real `.expo/types/router.d.ts` with the two new routes, making the local sandbox patch above moot the moment she does this); (4) live-check the Reset card, the share composer's video/audio 60s cap, and the Gallery's "Show more" pagination on both web and her phone, since none of this has been visually verified outside static code review. "Care for yourself" and Rediscover remain fully unbuilt — explicitly deferred to a future session using this same draft → clarify → lock → build → verify pattern.

## Nourish postpartum weeks 43-137 — re-ingested from the correct reworked batches, 2026-09-15 (fixes a real toddler-centric-content bug live for one day)

**What went wrong.** The morning after the Nourish series went live (see the section directly below), Roop live-checked the website and caught postpartum week 119's Nourish card showing "How much magnesium does your toddler need" — clearly written to help a mother feed her toddler, not a meal plan for the mother herself. She remembered correctly: back on 2026-09-05 through 2026-09-13, she and Claude had discovered this exact deviation (the series drifting from mother-centric meal plans into toddler-nutrition-education around week 43+) and spent many sessions reworking postpartum weeks 43 onward, batch by batch, back to mother-centric — see `Nourish - Batch 1 REWORK (Weeks 43, 56, 59, 60, 67, 82)` through `Batch 19 LOCKED (Weeks 151-157)` in the `Claude outputs` folder, and the two checkpoint commits `0d9bdb1`/`6bb35ac`.

**Root cause, confirmed by checking file timestamps, not just filenames.** The Nourish ingestion built on 2026-09-14 (below) prioritized the **individual per-week root files** (`Nourish - Postpartum Week N (LOCKED).docx`) over multi-week batch files, on the reasonable assumption that an individually-locked file is the more specific, more current source. That assumption was wrong for weeks 43-137: those individual files predate the rework-by-batch project by one to three weeks and were **never updated** after the batches fixed them — they're stale, pre-rework copies sitting right next to their own correction. Confirmed directly: the individual `Week 119 (LOCKED).docx` is dated 2026-09-02 and is the toddler-centric magnesium version (29 mentions of "toddler"); `Batch 13 LOCKED (Weeks 115-120).docx` is dated a week later, 2026-09-09, and correctly covers potassium for the mother, no toddler content at all. Naming also isn't a reliable signal of which file is final — some batches (1-8) have only a `REWORK` file with no subsequent relock; others (9-19) went through several `REWORK v1/v2/v3...` rounds before a final `LOCKED` save that postdates all of them. Determined the true final file per batch by comparing actual file modification times, not by assuming "LOCKED" or the highest version number is automatically last.

**Fix.** Re-parsed all 19 batch files (same parser built for the original ingestion, unchanged — the batch docx structure matches what it already expects) and compared every week against what was live. 114 weeks total exist across the 19 batches (postpartum 43-157, with week 58 genuinely absent from any batch — checked its individual file directly, it was already mother-centric and never needed reworking, so that's a real non-gap, not an oversight). Of those 114, **93 weeks (43-137, excluding 58 and 84) actually differed** from what had just gone live; the other 21 (138-157, plus 84) were already correct, since those either had no individual file to wrongly take priority, or Batch 7's week 84 happened to match already. Re-scanned the corrected 93 weeks' actual meal-chart fields (breakfast/lunch/nourishment-break/dinner, notes, titles) for any remaining toddler-centric language — clean; the only two remaining "toddler" mentions are in week *themes* ("...as your toddler starts daycare," "...when your toddler becomes a picky eater"), which are legitimately mother-centric situational topics, not the child's own meal plan.

Generated as `supabase/migration_58_nourish_postpartum_rework_fix_1of6.sql` through `_6of6.sql` (same per-row-statement, ~260-280KB-chunk pattern established for the original migrations 55-57, each independently validated with `pglast` before handoff). All six run in Supabase, confirmed live — spot-checked week 119 directly: `theme_title` now reads "Potassium, a mineral worth knowing about beyond bananas," not magnesium/toddler.

**Worth remembering for any future content-ingestion task in this project:** when both an individually-locked file and a later multi-week batch file exist for the same content, don't assume the individual file is more current just because it's more specific — check actual file modification timestamps for both, every time. This is the same lesson already logged once before for Nourish Weeks 83-84 (a locked file + a later, more substantive rework existing side by side) — now confirmed as a recurring risk across this entire content series, not a one-off, and worth checking explicitly on any future Nourish (or other locked-content) ingestion rather than trusting file-priority assumptions from a single point in time.

## Nourish weekly meal-plan series — connected to the Care module, 2026-09-14

**Resolves a real confusion, worth reading if either "Nourish" system ever comes up again.** Roop lived-tested the native Care Chart and reported the Nourish card missing/thin on several weeks. Investigation (see the 2026-09-14 session) confirmed this project has always had **two completely separate "Nourish" systems** that happen to share a name:

1. **The Care Chart's own `care_chart_week_content.nourish` field** — a single short plain-text sentence per week, authored directly inside the Care Chart SQL migrations (migrations 33-47). This is the one with the confirmed, still-unfixed placeholder-text gap for postpartum weeks 79-156 ("No specific note this week." — 338 occurrences, documented elsewhere in this file). Unrelated to item 2 below.
2. **The standalone Nourish docx-per-week nutrition series** — a much richer weekly nutrition guide (theme, why-it-matters, condition notes, a full 7-day meal chart with A/B choices for breakfast/lunch/nourishment-break/dinner), one `.docx` per week, drafted and reviewed over many sessions per this file's own "Nourish content series" entry. Until this session, this series had **no database ingestion pipeline at all** — every locked week lived purely as a `.docx` file at the folder root, completely disconnected from the live app.

Roop's instruction once this was explained: connect series #2 into the app for real, so every mother sees her actual day's real meal plan inside the Care Chart's Nourish card — not the thinner field from #1.

**What's covered.** The series runs pregnancy weeks 1-40 and postpartum weeks 1-157 (six phases, Early healing through "Your rhythm, year three"). **Pregnancy weeks 2-8 have no locked file anywhere in the project folder** — checked the folder root, the `Claude outputs` batch folder, and the (empty) `locked-content` folder; this is a real, standing content gap, not a session-mount artifact (the same gap was flagged once before, in the Week 33/34 Nourish-series entries below, and still hasn't been filled). Flagged to Roop directly; her call was to launch without those 7 weeks rather than block on drafting or hunting for them — those weeks fall back to the Care Chart's own thinner `nourish` field in the meantime. Everything else (33 pregnancy weeks + all 157 postpartum weeks = 190 weeks total) is live.

**Schema — `supabase/migration_54_nourish_week_content_schema.sql`.** New table `nourish_week_content`, keyed by `(stage, week_number)` — **not** the same numbering as `care_chart_week_content`'s `journey_week_number`. This series is authored and reviewed in ordinary gestational weeks (1-40, "I'm 18 weeks pregnant") and ordinary weeks-since-birth (1-157, "my baby is 6 weeks old"), so a new calculator (`src/lib/nourishCalculator.ts`, ported verbatim to `mobile/lib/nourishCalculator.ts`) computes a mother's real stage/week/day-of-week straight from her `due_date`/`baby_dob`, independent of `journeyWeekNumber`. Day-of-week is anchored to her own due date (pregnancy) or baby's DOB (postpartum), so "Day 1" of any week lines up consistently for her specifically, not to the calendar. Read-only RLS for authenticated users, same pattern as every other content table. Also added `user_nourish_choices` (which A/B option she picked each day) — schema only, not yet wired into any UI; a reasonable next step if Roop wants the choice to persist, not built this pass since the ask was "show the plan," not "track her picks."

**Content — `migration_55_nourish_week_content_pregnancy.sql`, `migration_56_nourish_week_content_postpartum_part1.sql`, `migration_57_nourish_week_content_postpartum_part2.sql`.** Parsed programmatically (not hand-transcribed) from pandoc-converted docx text — a custom parser (built this session, not persisted anywhere reusable yet — would need rebuilding in a future session if more weeks are added later, same caveat pattern as the Nourish drafting toolchain elsewhere in this file) that handles the real format drift across ~19 months of iteration (bold vs. plain headings, blockquote-wrapped vs. plain A/B options, escaped punctuation, a leaked internal "not mother-facing" implementation note caught and stripped from Trimester Week 10's Day 2). Individual locked per-week files (weeks 1-137 postpartum, most pregnancy weeks) took priority over multi-week batch files (`Claude outputs/Nourish - Batch 16/17/18/19 LOCKED...docx`, covering postpartum weeks 138-157, which haven't been split into individual per-week files yet). All 190 rows validated: real Postgres SQL parse (`pglast`), balanced quotes/parens, zero parse errors, spot-checked content across eras (early pregnancy, late pregnancy, individual-file postpartum, batch-file postpartum) reads correctly.

**Code.** `src/lib/nourishCalculator.ts` / `mobile/lib/nourishCalculator.ts` (the stage/week/day calculator, described above). `src/types/nourishContent.ts` (shared row/day shape, web only — native duplicates the type inline in `care.tsx`, matching this project's existing "copy-paste safe, keep in sync" pattern for pure logic/types). `src/components/CareWeekContent.tsx` and `mobile/app/(tabs)/care.tsx` both gained a `NourishMealCard` — shown in place of the old plain-sentence Nourish card whenever a row exists for her computed stage/week: theme + mantra, **today's day only** (Roop's explicit choice over showing the full week, so a mother can glance once and know what to eat right now), all four meal slots with both A/B choices visible, a "still hungry" suggestion, the week's own short Care Chart `nourish` sentence retained underneath as a small supplementary note, and a collapsed "more about this week" section (why it matters, meat/fish/eggs guidance, condition notes, portion-size guidance). Falls back to the exact old behavior for the pregnancy-weeks-2-8 gap or if she hasn't set a due date/DOB yet. Verified clean on `npx tsc --noEmit` (both web and mobile) and `npx eslint`/`npx expo lint` (zero new issues — every flagged line on the native side is the same pre-existing unescaped-apostrophe baseline documented elsewhere in this file).

**Status: code built and verified this session, NOT yet deployed.** Still needed from Roop: (1) run all four migrations in Supabase's SQL editor, in order — `migration_54` (schema) then `55`/`56`/`57` (content, order doesn't matter between these three); (2) push the code via GitHub Desktop (web: `CareWeekContent.tsx`, `nourishCalculator.ts`, `nourishContent.ts`, `chart/page.tsx`; native: `care.tsx`, `nourishCalculator.ts`) and let Vercel redeploy; (3) sync the native change to her phone the same way as always (`npm install` isn't needed — no new dependency — just re-running `npx expo start` and reloading in Expo Go). Not yet seen live on her phone or the website.

**Worth a follow-up decision, not resolved this session:** the pregnancy-weeks-2-8 gap — draft them fresh, or track down whether they exist somewhere else first. Also worth considering whether `user_nourish_choices` (built but unwired) is worth surfacing as a real "which did you pick" toggle in a future pass, matching the tappable-checkbox pattern used elsewhere in Care.

## Expo Go SDK mismatch recurred, 2026-09-06 — fixed by sideloading, not upgrading

Roop hit "Project is incompatible with this version of Expo Go" again (project on SDK 54, her phone's Play-Store Expo Go had auto-updated to SDK-57-only — the same category of issue as the original 2026-07-31 fix, just in the opposite direction this time: her installed client moved past the project instead of behind it). Deliberately did NOT upgrade the project to SDK 57 to match — a full SDK bump touches 15+ native dependencies (reanimated/worklets/gesture-handler/svg/image-picker/av/file-system, etc.) with no device or emulator in this sandbox to verify against, real risk to a project with months of working, tested functionality. Instead had her sideload the SDK-54-compatible Expo Go client via the error screen's own "Learn how to install Expo Go for SDK 54" link (expo.dev/go) — a phone-side-only fix, zero code/project risk. Worth remembering: this can recur any time Play Store updates her Expo Go app in the background, since Google eventually stops distributing support for older SDKs through the main listing. If it keeps happening, upgrading the whole project to whatever SDK is currently store-current becomes the more durable fix — but that's a real, careful task (verify every native dependency's compatible version, ideally with real device access to test), not a quick one.

## Git lock file blocked a GitHub Desktop commit, 2026-09-06 — fixed

GitHub Desktop's commit for the 263 pending Nourish files failed with "A lock file already exists in the repository, which blocks this operation from completing." Found and removed a stale, empty `.git/index.lock` (dated all the way back to 2026-08-12 — leftover from some earlier interrupted git operation, not anything from this session). Cleared via `rm .git/index.lock`; `git status` ran clean immediately after. If this happens again: check for `.git/index.lock` (or any `.git/*.lock` file) and delete it — it's always safe to remove once you're sure no other git process is actually running at that moment.

## Nourish content series — week-by-week nutrition drafting, in progress, updated 2026-08-25 (Trimester series: Weeks 1-40 locked, complete; Postpartum series: Weeks 1-82 locked, Weeks 83-84 in DRAFT v1 awaiting review)

**What this is.** "Nourish" is a separate content-drafting project from the native-app/website work documented elsewhere in this file: a week-by-week nutrition series for the Mom's Village app, one Word document (.docx) per week, each covering a single nutrient theme structured as a 7-day meal-choice "chart." This has been running across several sessions and is NOT yet reflected anywhere else in this file — this is its first entry. Files live at `/Users/roop/Downloads/mom-village/Nourish - [Trimester] Week N (LOCKED).docx` for every locked week; the working build scripts and drafts live in the sandbox at `/tmp/nourish/` (bash path `/sessions/.../mnt/...` doesn't apply to `/tmp` — it's local to whichever sandbox session is running, NOT persisted across sessions/window changes). **Important: `/tmp/nourish/`'s build scripts and intermediate drafts do not survive a new conversation/session** — only the actual locked `.docx` files saved into the real `mom-village` folder persist. A fresh session starting this work will need to recreate the build-script infrastructure from scratch (see "Technical setup" below) — it cannot resume from `/tmp/nourish/`.

**Status as of this entry: Weeks 1-23 are locked and saved** in `/Users/roop/Downloads/mom-village/` as individual `Nourish - [Trimester] Week N (LOCKED).docx` files (see the folder for the exact set). Week 23 (omega-3 fats / DHA) was the most recently locked, this session, at an 8.8/10 score on v2 after one full revision round (v1 6.9/10 → v2 8.8/10 with a final small set of corrections → locked directly off that "should then be ready to lock" verdict, per the standing rule that this phrasing counts as an explicit lock signal). Week 23's real content lessons, worth carrying into every future week: (1) every main eating moment (breakfast/lunch/nourishment break/dinner) across Days 1-6 needs genuine A/B alternatives — don't let some meals go single-option, that was v1's biggest structural miss; (2) when a week's A/B choice is specifically illustrating a direct-source-vs-precursor distinction (like direct DHA vs. ALA), label it once inline ("Direct-DHA option:" / "Plant omega-3 option:") rather than repeating the underlying science caveat at every meal — the explanation lives once in "Why it matters now"; (3) don't over-rely on one convenient non-veg ingredient (egg) as the B option in nearly every slot — vary it (chicken in different formats: sandwich/roll, keema, stew, curry, pulao; fish only within the 2-3/week cap; curd/paneer+an ALA food is an acceptable B-lite option for a snack slot when another animal option isn't needed) and aim for no more than one egg-based moment per day; (4) never name a generic/unspecified fish species (e.g. "mackerel" alone) — some species within a genus are on mercury-avoid lists while others aren't; use "sardines or another fish confirmed to be a lower-mercury choice" instead; (5) don't extend a population-specific (e.g. US) survey finding to Indian mothers without a named, citable Indian source — use a general formulation instead; (6) specify ground (not whole/roasted) flaxseed, and give flaxseed oil explicit "stir in after cooking, don't use for high-heat cooking" handling; (7) any milk-based overnight-soak preparation (e.g. chia pudding) must specify pasteurized milk and refrigeration, never imply room-temperature overnight sitting; (8) don't promise a specific serving-count-to-milligram equivalence for a nutrient target unless a source actually supports that exact conversion — describe fish as contributing "substantially toward" a target instead. **Week 24 (magnesium) drafted, v1 presented for review, same session as Week 23's lock.** Applied every Week 23 lesson above from v1: full A/B alternatives on every meal across Days 1-6 (Day 7 free-choice), no more than ~1 egg-based B option per day (rotated chicken formats — stew, curry, keema, pulao — instead), no vague/unspecified species issue (not fish-relevant this week), no unsourced population claims, and no serving-count-to-mg equivalence promise. Magnesium's real story turned out to be the opposite direction from omega-3: it's a plant-forward nutrient (leafy greens, legumes, nuts, seeds, whole grains are the best sources; meat/fish/eggs contribute comparatively modest amounts) — the "If you eat meat, fish, or eggs" callout says so honestly rather than reusing omega-3's animal-concentrated framing. ICMR-NIN 2020 gives a general adult women's RDA (370mg/day) but no pregnancy-specific figure — same gap pattern as fiber/choline/omega-3 — so NIH's pregnancy AI (350-360mg/day) is used as the labeled international reference. The leg-cramp question (something Indian mothers ask about often) is addressed with real, honestly-hedged mixed/low-quality Cochrane evidence rather than oversold. No condition-specific note this week (type-2-diabetes glycemic-control evidence exists but isn't gestational-diabetes-specific and ADA says evidence is insufficient anyway, so no diabetes_gd note was manufactured) — zero condition notes, consistent with recent-week restraint. Week 24's own "Looking ahead" line names **calcium** as next — Claude's best guess, not verified against Weeks 1-22 (which stay unopened per the standing rule) — confirm with Roop or double-check before treating it as settled. **Week 24 — LOCKED and saved, same session.** v2 scored 9.2/10 on ChatGPT's second pass, with four small final corrections (RDA sentence clarity, two remaining B-path magnesium gaps on Day 5, a clearer "excess intake is a supplement issue, not a food issue" framing on Day 6, and a less over-reassuring Day 7 closing note) — applied directly and locked off that "should be ready to lock" verdict, per the standing rule. Saved as `Nourish - Second Trimester Week 24 (LOCKED).docx`. Week 24's own "Looking ahead" line names **calcium** as next — still Claude's guess, unconfirmed against Weeks 1-22.

**Week 24 v2 (context, superseded by the lock above) was built after ChatGPT's v1 review (6.4/10 — not ready to lock).** Real content lessons from this round, worth carrying forward: (1) don't imply a benefit (e.g. sleep, blood pressure) just because a nutrient participates in the relevant biological process — muscle/nerve function participation doesn't establish a sleep claim, and general-adult BP-trial data doesn't transfer to a pregnancy-specific claim; (2) when evidence is genuinely conflicting (e.g. magnesium for leg cramps), describe the conflict and explicitly say not to act on it (self-diagnose or self-supplement), rather than framing it as "reasonable to try"; (3) "plant sources are naturally abundant" is a different, weaker claim than "diets already cover this comfortably" — don't let a food-source fact slide into an adequacy assurance; (4) audit every B option specifically for whether it silently drops the week's target nutrient (e.g. "oats+almonds" becoming "oats+egg" without the almonds) — this is the same A/B-parity discipline as Week 23 but applied at the ingredient level, not just the meal-slot level; (5) don't name a dish format (e.g. "pulao") paired with an incompatible base grain (e.g. "whole-wheat") — check that named dishes are realistic; (6) when a source is optional/flavor-only (e.g. jaggery in porridge), say so explicitly rather than stating it as a fixed ingredient; (7) if two NIH fact sheets (or other primary sources) cite different specific numbers for the same claim (e.g. "300" vs "600+" enzyme systems), don't pick one silently — use a vaguer, defensible formulation instead; (8) verify AI vs. RDA terminology precisely against the source table's own footnotes, don't assume a life-stage-specific figure is an AI just because some nearby rows are.

**Week 25 — a real topic-tracking bug was caught and fixed before drafting, new session, 2026-08-05.** Week 24's own "Looking ahead" line named calcium as next, but per the standing warning already logged above ("Claude's best guess, not verified against Weeks 1-22... confirm with Roop or double-check before treating it as settled"), a fresh session actually did that check this time — by pandoc-converting every locked file's real "Weekly nourishment theme" text rather than trusting any single week's closing line. Result: **calcium was already fully covered in Week 9** ("Calcium, built in steadily"), so Week 25 could not be calcium. Separately, and unfixable now since both are already locked and presented to Roop without her having caught it either: **Week 24 (magnesium) turned out to be an unintentional repeat of Week 16** ("Magnesium — real roles, honest limits"). Given this, **checking the full locked-week theme list for a genuine duplicate before drafting is now a required step, not optional, whenever a "Looking ahead" line's topic can't be independently corroborated** — don't rely on a single prior week's closing line alone. Iodine was chosen instead for Week 25 (never covered, ICMR-NIN 2020 has a real general-adult figure for it, strong India-specific content available via NIDDCP/salt-iodization). This was flagged to Roop transparently before drafting, not silently substituted.

Week 25 (iodine) went through three review rounds: v1 8.3/10 (8 corrections: don't imply iodized salt automatically meets pregnancy needs, don't overstate prenatal-supplement iodine coverage, correct fetal-thyroid timing so the baby doesn't read as fully independent of the mother by mid-pregnancy, replace a vague/under-sourced "national survey" population claim with FSSAI's actual iodized-salt requirement, fix an egg-frequency contradiction across several B options, correct an internal seafood-day-count error, reduce repeated exact meal combinations across days, and soften a thyroid-condition note so it doesn't override individualized medical guidance) → v2 9.3/10 (3 required fixes: remove a remaining "no benefit to extra iodine" absolute claim sitting next to an acknowledgment that prenatal iodine content varies, soften "iodine breaks down" to the more accurate "iodine can be lost" for the storage/cooking day, correct the Week 26 preview so it doesn't imply B12 matters less for mothers who do eat animal food; plus two optional chicken-repetition substitutions) → v3 9.7/10, explicit "lock" verdict, locked directly. **Week 25 lessons, worth carrying into every future week:** (1) always verify a "Looking ahead"-inferred topic against the full set of locked-week themes, not just trust the single prior week's line, since a real duplicate has now happened twice (calcium at Week 9, magnesium at Week 16 vs. 24); (2) a nutrient tied to one dominant, easy-to-name food source (like iodized salt) needs repeated, explicit "this is an important source, not an automatic-adequacy guarantee" framing everywhere the food is mentioned, not just once in the intro — the same overclaim tendency recurred in at least four separate places in Week 25's v1 before being caught; (3) never claim a supplement (e.g. a prenatal) reliably contains a nutrient without a source confirming that — "not every prenatal contains X, check the label" is the safe default phrasing, already established for choline/omega-3 and now reinforced again for iodine; (4) watch for a later-arriving sentence that quietly re-introduces an absolute claim ("no benefit to extra X") right next to an already-corrected nuanced one ("not every prenatal has X") — these two can end up contradicting each other even across separate correction rounds if not re-read together; (5) an unsourced/vague population claim ("national surveys show...") should be replaced with the single most specific, real, citable regulatory fact available (here, FSSAI's actual iodized-salt requirement) rather than softened into something still vague; (6) condition-specific notes must defer to the mother's own care team's existing plan rather than issue a general directive ("keep eating X as normal") that could contradict individualized medical advice; (7) for a nutrient that's genuinely diet-neutral (matches iodine, hydration), don't force an animal-vs-plant framing onto the "If you eat meat, fish, or eggs" callout — say plainly that it doesn't split sharply along that line; (8) watch for one non-egg protein (chicken, in this case) becoming the new default overused B-option once egg frequency is corrected — variety discipline has to be re-checked after every fix, not assumed to transfer.

**Week 26 (vitamin B12) — LOCKED and LIVE, 2026-08-05, same session as Week 25.** Went through two review rounds: v1 7.9/10 (8 corrections: don't present an ICMR-NIN pregnancy increment that isn't confirmed in the primary summary table — general adult figure only, US RDA as the pregnancy reference; correct the plant-source claim so fortified foods are described as fortified non-animal sources, not "the only plant-based sources"; add a genuine, practical vegan/dairy-free pathway, since every meal option had depended on dairy and B12 doesn't occur in any plant food at all; replace a single "roughly two-thirds" India prevalence figure with the real 40-70% range and drop the blanket blood-test suggestion; remove an inaccurate "your body simply excretes it" explanation for the lack of an upper limit; soften the metformin note so it doesn't imply routine testing for every short-term user, and fix a raw internal flag name ("diabetes_gd") leaking into mother-facing text; broaden the breastfeeding-risk explanation beyond low intake to include malabsorption; reduce dairy saturation in the nourishment breaks) → v2 9.2/10 (3 final corrections: remove internal project language — "outside scope" — that had leaked into the mother-facing vegan box; fix an awkward double-conjunction sentence and soften two slightly-absolute lines; fix a bad final-page break stranding the food-safety section's first bullet alone on its own page) → locked directly off the "should be ready to lock" verdict, per the standing rule. **Week 26 lessons, worth carrying into every future week:** (1) an unverified secondary-source figure (here, a claimed ICMR-NIN pregnancy increment cited by several secondary sources but not visible in the primary summary table) must not be presented as confirmed — when only a general adult figure is confirmed against the primary source, say so, and use a clearly-labelled international reference for pregnancy rather than adding an unconfirmed increment; (2) for a nutrient that's genuinely absent from an entire dietary pattern (B12 and plant foods), a written pathway for that mother isn't optional polish — it has to actually name what does and doesn't count (fortified-labelled products count, generic "healthy plant foods" don't) and point to a real next step (checking her prenatal, asking her team); (3) watch for internal/developer framing language ("outside scope," "not mother-facing," etc.) accidentally leaking into content meant for the mother, not just factual errors — a wording pass should specifically check for this; (4) a technical/internal flag name (e.g. a database value like "diabetes_gd") must never render as mother-facing label text — condition-note labels need to read as plain language regardless of what the underlying flag is called; (5) rendering the actual document and checking real page breaks (not just assuming five or six pages "look fine") is necessary — a stranded single bullet on its own page is a real, catchable defect; (6) even a "small final polish" round can still contain a real factual walk-back (the pregnancy-increment removal was substantive, not cosmetic) — don't assume a low corrections-count means the round is purely cosmetic.

**Week 27 (potassium) — LOCKED and LIVE, 2026-08-05, same session as Week 26.** Went through two review rounds: v1 7.8/10 (7 corrections: remove an unverified ICMR-NIN attribution for a figure — 3510mg/day — that actually turned out to be a WHO general-adult recommendation, not ICMR's and not pregnancy-specific, replaced with the correctly-labelled US pregnancy Adequate Intake; fix real menu repetition — poha, orange juice, rajma, palak dal, banana, potato, and coconut water all recurring across the week; replace repeated orange juice with whole oranges and add a blood-glucose condition note so juice/dried fruit/coconut water aren't presented as unrestricted; fix two A/B pairs where an egg had silently replaced the week's featured fruit instead of sitting alongside it; fix a day where selecting every B option could produce three separate egg-based meals; rewrite an overly absolute "potassium is just excreted" line and an overly broad high-blood-pressure warning into an accurate, appropriately narrow kidney-disease/medication-and-supplement caution; soften a rigid "potato skin must stay on" instruction) → v2 9.4/10 (2 small corrections: fix an inaccurate internal verification-note claim that every milk-based item specified pasteurized milk, when only explicit glasses of milk did — curd/buttermilk rely on the standing food-safety footer instead; strip the changelog/verification block before final pagination so pages don't split awkwardly around a heading or closing line, since draft-stage internal material was distorting the real page count; plus an optional wording polish removing an awkward double-"if" in one condition label) → locked directly off the explicit "I would be comfortable marking Week 27 LOCKED" verdict. **Week 27 lessons, worth carrying into every future week:** (1) a figure repeated across multiple secondary sources as "ICMR-NIN's" can still turn out to belong to a different body entirely (WHO, in this case) — attributing a real, correct number to the wrong source is its own distinct error from inventing a number, and needs the same primary-source scrutiny; (2) a week's own single "signature" food (bananas, here) can silently become the only fruit used across breakfast, snacks, and the reflection line unless fruit variety is deliberately tracked across all seven days, the same discipline already applied to vegetables/proteins; (3) letting an egg replace the week's featured food in a B option (rather than sit alongside it) reintroduces the exact "B option silently drops the target nutrient" bug from earlier weeks, just via addition/substitution rather than a missing ingredient; (4) always trace every day's full set of B options together, not just one meal at a time, when checking a "no more than one egg-based moment per day" rule — a day can look fine meal-by-meal and still allow three eggs if every B is picked; (5) a safety caution should name the actual narrow risk (concentrated supplements/salt substitutes, specific medical conditions) rather than a broad proxy condition (e.g. high blood pressure in general) that doesn't itself make ordinary food unsafe; (6) draft-stage internal notes (changelog, verification basis) can materially distort how pagination looks mid-review — real final-page-break judgment should wait until that material is actually stripped, not be assessed against the draft's own page count.

**Week 28 (vitamin A) — LOCKED and LIVE, 2026-08-05, same session as Week 27. A real trimester-boundary bug was caught and fixed mid-draft.** This project's own running convention had only ever specified "Weeks 1-13 = First Trimester, Weeks 14+ = Second Trimester" with no stated end boundary — exactly the drift risk CLAUDE.md itself had flagged ("confirm against the real pregnancy-week mapping if this ever seems to drift, rather than assuming the pattern holds indefinitely into Third Trimester"). ChatGPT's v1 review caught it for real: under ACOG's standard definition, the third trimester begins at 28 weeks 0 days, so **Week 28 is the first Third Trimester week, not Second** — corrected in the title, file name, and Day 4's pregnancy-timing text. **Confirmed trimester boundary going forward: Weeks 1-13 = First Trimester, Weeks 14-27 = Second Trimester, Weeks 28+ = Third Trimester.** Weeks 14-27 remain correctly labelled (all genuinely fall within the real second trimester) — nothing already locked needs revisiting.

Went through two review rounds: v1 7.1/10 (9 corrections: fix the trimester label described above; remove an overstated "majority of the population" India deficiency claim built on self-reported-symptom data that couldn't be responsibly generalized to a national figure — replaced with a calm "mention a real symptom to your team, don't self-diagnose" message instead; give both age-specific pregnancy upper limits for preformed vitamin A, not just one; remove a confusing ICMR-general-adult-vs-US-pregnancy figure comparison from mother-facing text entirely, since placing a higher general figure next to a lower pregnancy figure invited exactly the wrong conclusion; soften absolute beta-carotene claims like "however much you eat"; strengthen the liver caution to also name cod liver oil and unprescribed retinol supplements, matching real official pregnancy guidance; fix the "most prenatals already contain a safe amount" line, since formulations vary; perform a full cross-week variety audit and rebuild most of the week's meals, since it had reused a large share of Week 27's actual dish vocabulary almost verbatim; fix four A/B pairs where the featured vitamin-A food had been dropped rather than carried into the non-vegetarian option) → v2 9.2/10 (4 small refinements: remove one remaining absolute "entirely safe" claim; make the Day 7 closing note and the theme's own mantra quote consistent with the softened, non-absolute language used everywhere else; reduce sweet-potato appearing on four separate days down to two, and swap out a breakfast that was itself too close to Week 27's actual oats-porridge breakfast; correct an internal changelog line that overstated how many days were "genuinely different" when a couple of the new dishes still repeated within the week or against Week 27) → locked directly off the "After these four refinements, Week 28 can be locked" verdict, per the standing rule.

**Week 28 lessons, worth carrying into every future week:** (1) a running convention stated as open-ended ("Weeks 14+ = X") is a real drift risk, not just a hypothetical one flagged for later — it should be actively re-checked against the real, external standard (ACOG's trimester boundaries) at the point a series crosses a plausible boundary, not just left as a standing warning; (2) for a "both too little and too much matter" nutrient, deficiency-prevalence claims need an even higher evidentiary bar than usual — self-reported-symptom survey data is not the same claim as confirmed biochemical deficiency, and shouldn't be generalized to "the population" without the specific study's age group, region, and population type attached; (3) placing two correctly-sourced numbers side by side (a general-adult figure and a lower pregnancy-specific figure from a different body) can itself create a false impression even when neither number is wrong — sometimes the fix is removing a comparison entirely from mother-facing text, not just correcting the labels; (4) an upper-limit or safety-ceiling claim should include every age band a source provides (here, both the 19-50 and 14-18 figures), not just the more commonly-cited one; (5) a specific food-based caution (liver) should be checked against real official guidance for whether related items (cod liver oil, unprescribed high-dose supplements) belong in the same caution, rather than assuming the single most obvious example covers the full picture; (6) a full-week meal rebuild still needs its own internal cross-check for repetition — fixing repetition against the previous week doesn't guarantee the new dishes don't repeat against each other or reuse a "signature" ingredient (carrot, sweet potato) more than necessary; (7) an internal verification-note claim about what was "rebuilt" or "fixed" should be re-read against the final content one more time before presenting, since it's easy for the note to overstate the completeness of a fix made mid-revision.

**Week 29 (vitamin E) — LOCKED and LIVE, 2026-08-05, same session as Week 28.** ICMR-NIN's 2020 summary tables (Tables 3 and 4, the same primary source used since Week 23) were checked directly and confirmed not to include a vitamin E figure at all — handled the same honest way as fiber/choline: no invented or borrowed ICMR number, NIH's US pregnancy RDA (15 mg/day) used as the clearly-labelled international reference instead. Went through two review rounds: v1 7.3/10 (8 corrections: add the adolescent supplemental upper limit — 800 mg/day, ages 14-18 — alongside the adult 1,000 mg/day figure; remove an unsupported "~300mg" interaction threshold NIH doesn't actually specify precisely, replace with WHO's real pregnancy-relevant guidance and soften the aspirin mention with explicit "don't stop any prescribed medicine" reassurance; remove an overconfident "comfortably covers vitamin E" adequacy claim; distinguish stronger vitamin-E food sources — sunflower seeds, almonds, peanuts, sunflower/safflower oil — from genuinely weaker ones like walnuts and sesame, rather than treating every nut/seed as equal; remove an unsupported mustard-vs-groundnut-oil ranking not backed by a directly-applicable Indian food-composition source; rebuild the week's meals with a real cross-week variety audit against Weeks 27 *and* 28 together, not just the immediately prior week, since peanuts/fish/sunflower seeds/egg-in-breakfast-B were repeating almost every meal; add a nut/seed allergy substitution line, given how frequently nuts and seeds now appear across the week; simplify overreaching function language) → v2 9.2/10 (3 final corrections: tighten the WHO wording so it reads as one combined vitamin-E-plus-vitamin-C antioxidant recommendation rather than two separate universal claims; audit and fix each day's full all-B selectable path, not just each meal slot individually — Day 3's all-B path had breakfast+lunch+dinner all animal-protein, and Day 6's all-B path was three chicken moments including "chicken curry" repeated twice in one day; tighten the allergy note so it can't read as encouraging experimentation with a different nut/seed after a known allergy) → locked directly off the "After these three changes, Week 29 can be locked" verdict, per the standing rule. Saved as `Nourish - Third Trimester Week 29 (LOCKED).docx`. Week 29's own "Looking ahead" line names **selenium** as next — Claude's best guess, not yet verified against the full locked-week theme list; confirm before treating it as settled, per the standing rule below.

**Week 29 lessons, worth carrying into every future week:** (1) when a nutrient has no confirmed ICMR-NIN 2020 figure, actually re-check the primary source's summary tables directly each time rather than trusting a prior session's memory of what they contain — this was reconfirmed by re-fetching the same nin.res.in PDF already used for Weeks 23-28 rather than assuming vitamin E's absence from memory; (2) a numeric interaction/safety threshold (like a "megadose = X mg" claim) needs the same primary-source precision as an RDA — if NIH itself says the exact threshold is unknown or imprecise, don't manufacture a specific number to sound more concrete, state the uncertainty and use the strongest actually-precise guidance available instead (WHO's actual recommendation, here); (3) naming a specific medication (aspirin) in a caution needs an explicit "don't stop your prescribed medicine because of this" reassurance in the same breath, especially when it's a medication commonly prescribed *during* pregnancy itself (low-dose aspirin), not just an incidental interacting drug; (4) not every food source within a nutrient category contributes equally — grouping "nuts and seeds" as interchangeable anchors overstates weaker contributors (walnuts, sesame, cashews) and can make menu variety look more nutritionally meaningful than it is; distinguish strong from weak sources explicitly; (5) a comparative ranking between specific foods/oils (mustard vs. groundnut) needs a directly-applicable source for the specific regional product being compared, not a generic food-composition database standing in for it — when that source isn't available, remove the ranking entirely rather than soften it; (6) a cross-week meal-variety audit must check against at least the two immediately preceding weeks, not just the single most recent one — a dish can cleanly clear Week 28 and still duplicate Week 27 (caught here: "besan cheela" vs. Week 27's "besan chilla," the same dish under alternate spelling); (7) when many meals in a week share a category of ingredient (nuts/seeds, here), an allergy substitution note stops being optional polish and becomes a real safety gap if omitted — and the note itself must not read as license to try a different allergen, only to substitute with something already known-safe; (8) auditing "no more than one egg-based moment per day" or "no meat-every-meal" must be done by tracing each day's full all-A and all-B selectable path end-to-end, not meal-slot-by-meal-slot — a day can pass every individual slot's check and still produce an all-meat or triple-protein day when a mother picks the same letter all day, which is a realistic real-world selection pattern.

**Week 30 (selenium) — LOCKED and LIVE, 2026-08-05, same session as Week 29.** Topic verified clear of duplication against the full list of 29 locked-week themes before drafting (per the standing rule) — selenium had never appeared. A genuinely confirmed ICMR-NIN 2020 figure exists this time (unlike vitamin E) but only surfaces in the full RDA/EAR report, not the shorter brief_note.pdf summary tables used since Week 23 — fetched the full report directly and found "The present Committee recommended 40 µg/day as adequate intake of selenium," a general-adult-only figure with no pregnancy-specific value, presented honestly alongside NIH's 60 mcg/day US pregnancy RDA.
