It is unrelated to the "Care Chart" postpartum weeks 0-6 / 7-12 / ... batches documented further down this file (see the "Postpartum weekly build" entries) — that is a separate content pipeline entirely: short structured database rows (`care_chart_week_content` table, `move`/`reset`/`nourish`/`hydration_goal` etc.) rendered in-app from SQL migrations, where `nourish` is a single plain-text field, not a full weekly document. Confirmed directly by reading the schema and migrations: there is currently no database ingestion pipeline for this docx-per-week Nourish series at all (no Nourish-specific migration exists) — every locked week lives purely as an individual `.docx` file at the folder root, the same way it always has. The postpartum series is organised into six named phases, mirroring (not literally sharing rows with) the Care Chart's own phase names: Phase 1 Early healing (Weeks 1-6), Phase 2 Finding rhythm (Weeks 7-12), Phase 3 Rebuilding (Weeks 13-26), Phase 4 Settling into strength (Weeks 27-52), Phase 5 Beyond the first year (Weeks 53 onward, currently in progress), Phase 6 not yet reached. Weeks 1-39 continued the Trimester series' single-nutrient-per-week structure (protein, vitamin C, zinc, calcium, vitamin D, iron, and on through the full micronutrient list); from Week 40 onward the series shifted to situational/life-stage topics (returning to work, sleep deprivation, picky eating, travel, weaning, allergies, and so on) rather than one-nutrient-per-week, reflecting that the micronutrient list was exhausted around the one-year mark.

**Full locked-theme index, Postpartum Weeks 1-82 (plus 83-84 in draft) — kept here specifically so a future session can check a candidate topic against the complete list, not just a "Looking ahead" guess, per the lesson learned the hard way at Trimester Week 25 (iodine nearly duplicated Week 9's calcium and Week 24 nearly duplicated Week 16's magnesium).** Phase 1, Early healing: W1 Refilling what birth asked of you; W2 More fuel, not less; W3 Protein, the quiet rebuilder; W4 Keeping things moving, gently; W5 Vitamin C; W6 Zinc. Phase 2, Finding rhythm: W7 Calcium; W8 Vitamin D; W9 Iron; W10 Iodine; W11 Omega-3s; W12 Vitamin B12. Phase 3, Rebuilding: W13 Folate; W14 Magnesium; W15 Choline; W16 Vitamin B6; W17 Vitamin A; W18 Vitamin E; W19 Thiamine; W20 Riboflavin; W21 Niacin; W22 Pantothenic acid; W23 Biotin; W24 Phosphorus; W25 Potassium; W26 Selenium. Phase 4, Settling into strength: W27 Vitamin K; W28 Manganese; W29 Copper; W30 Molybdenum; W31 Sodium; W32 Water; W33 Omega-6; W34 Carbohydrate; W35 Dietary fat; W36 Antioxidants; W37 Chloride; W38 Fluoride; W39 Multivitamins (why the prenatal bottle may no longer fit); W40 Back at work; W41 Running on little sleep; W42 When everyone has an opinion about what you should eat; W43 Eating together now that baby eats too; W44 When you can't think what to cook; W45 Feeding yourself without spending more; W46 When a fast is part of your faith; W47 Eating well when you're the one who's sick; W48 Eating well as breastfeeding changes; W49 Eating well as you return to movement; W50 Eating well if thinking about another pregnancy; W51 As your baby's first birthday approaches; W52 Completing your first year. Phase 5, Beyond the first year: W53 When the pressure to "get your body back" begins; W54 As your period returns; W55 As your toddler starts daycare; W56 When your toddler becomes a picky eater; W57 Through a postpartum thyroid swing; W58 When you're traveling with your family; W59 During festivals and celebrations; W60 When your child is sick; W61 While you're grieving; W62 As you start to feel like yourself again; W63 When you're expecting again; W64 When you're parenting solo for a stretch; W65 When you and your partner don't agree on how to feed your child; W66 Now that milk is starting to crowd out mealtimes; W67 As you wean your child off breastfeeding; W68 When your toddler is stuck on the same few foods; W69 When your toddler eats a vegetarian diet; W70 When your toddler's appetite drops; W71 When your toddler has a food allergy; W72 When your toddler is constipated; W73 When your toddler is teething; W74 Supporting your toddler's iron intake; W75 When your toddler has a stomach bug; W76 When your toddler has a cold; W77 As your toddler learns to feed themselves; W78 When your toddler is gaining weight slowly; W79 When your toddler is on antibiotics; W80 When a new sibling arrives; W81 Feeding an increasingly independent 18-month-old (retitled from an original "turning two" framing — see the Week 81 entry below); W82 Eating well while breastfeeding a toddler; W83 Eating well in hot weather (DRAFT v1, not yet locked); W84 Eating well when eating away from home (DRAFT v1, not yet locked). Weeks 1-78's detailed per-week review record (scores, specific corrections, lessons) predates what's available to the session performing this catch-up and isn't reconstructed here — the themes above are read directly from each locked file, so the list itself is reliable for duplicate-checking even without the review history behind each one.

**Week 81 (Feeding an increasingly independent 18-month-old) — a real age-calculation bug, worth flagging prominently.** This week was originally drafted around a "toddler turning two" framing, on the assumption that Postpartum Week 81 lines up with the second birthday. It does not: postpartum weeks and calendar months are not a 1:1 mapping (a month is ~4.345 weeks), so **Postpartum Week N corresponds to roughly N/4.345 months of the child's age** — Week 81 lands at roughly 18-19 months, not 24. The age-mismatched material (second-birthday-specific content) was pulled out and preserved rather than discarded, in `/home/claude/nourish_work/week104_holding_notes.md`, for reuse once the series actually reaches the week that lines up with age two (roughly Week 104 by the same conversion). Week 81 itself was rebuilt around the correct 18-19-month framing ("Feeding an increasingly independent 18-month-old") and locked. **Lesson, worth carrying into every future week that references a developmental age or milestone:** always compute N/4.345 and confirm the implied age before choosing or committing to any age-milestone-based topic — do not assume a round week number lines up with a round age.

**Week 82 (Eating well while breastfeeding a toddler) — LOCKED, this session.** Went through two review rounds: v1 7.8/10 (corrections: breastmilk's nutritional-contribution claim was overstated for this stage and needed softening; the maternal-calorie-intake advice was imprecise; the micronutrient list needed to be more complete; six separate toddler food-preparation/choking-hazard corrections were required — almond, watermelon, dates, guava, pomegranate, and chicken preparation all needed more specific safe-serving language; medication wording needed adjustment; milk-supply wording needed softening; a nursing-strike paragraph overstated a diagnosis it wasn't qualified to make; curd was repeated too often across the week; and the week needed an explicit fallback note for a mother who is not currently breastfeeding at all) → v2 9.5/10, with three final wording refinements (the mantra's anatomical framing, an implied-but-unintended "correct" weaning timeline that needed removing, and the ordering of the almond-butter meal option) plus a "fully cooked sprouted-moong cheela" wording fix (bringing it in line with the standing never-serve-raw-sprouts rule) and one Day-1 variety swap in the "if you're still hungry" line → locked directly off that verdict. **Week 82 lessons, worth carrying into every future week:** (1) when a week touches breastfeeding continuation, be precise that there is no routine medical cutoff and the decision stays with mother and child, without implying either continuing or stopping is the "more correct" choice; (2) always give an explicit fallback note for whichever caregiving situation the week's main framing doesn't cover (here: not currently breastfeeding); (3) toddler food-prep precision keeps expanding as new ingredients come up — this week added melon (thin soft pieces or lightly mashed, never round bite-sized balls) and confirmed chicken must be specified as boneless, cooked until tender, and finely shredded, not just "cooked."

**Weeks 83-84 — a structural correction driven by real user pushback, worth reading in full before drafting any future weather- or season-linked topic.** Week 83 was first drafted around "eating well in hot weather," reviewed at 5.8/10 with a fundamental objection: weather is not developmental, and a fixed postpartum week number can land in any season depending on the child's birth date and location, so a permanently weather-themed week doesn't work the way a nutrient or milestone week does. The initial fix attempt split this into two documents — a non-numbered "Hot Weather Bonus Guide" plus a fresh evergreen Week 83 topic ("Eating well when eating away from home") — carrying forward all of the reviewer's detailed safety corrections (a corrected food-storage/power-outage paragraph per CDC guidance, corrected heat-exhaustion-vs-heat-stroke guidance that no longer treats "no sweating" as a reliable rule-out sign, a qualified hydration figure, strengthened dehydration-sign language, removed overstated appetite-mechanism claims, toddler food-prep corrections for melon/cucumber/chicken, an ORS clarification, and reduced curd repetition). Roop then pushed back directly: "shouldn't we have v2 and why an additional document... we should add it to the master one as how will we upload on the app?" — correctly flagging that a two-document split doesn't obviously map onto anything the app can actually take. Rather than guessing at an answer, the session investigated the real `/Users/roop/Downloads/mom-village/` repository directly (`package.json`, `supabase/schema.sql` and migrations, `src/lib/library.ts`, this file) and confirmed: the app has no content-type for non-numbered "bonus" content, there is no existing database ingestion pipeline for the Nourish docx series at all (the per-week-locked-docx-in-the-folder model is the entire delivery mechanism right now, same as it's always been), and no master combined document is expected or maintained anywhere — each week is, and should stay, one standalone locked `.docx` file. Based on that, the two documents were restructured back into a clean two-numbered-week solution: Week 83 kept the hot-weather topic and all of its safety corrections, restored to standard numbered-week framing, with a disclaimer paragraph telling the mother to use the week's guidance whenever hot weather is actually happening where she lives and to return to it the next time a heatwave rolls around if the current weather doesn't match; the evergreen "eating away from home" topic moved to Week 84 instead. Both were rebuilt, re-verified (internal and cross-week duplicate checks, liver check, mantra presence, pagination with no mid-item page splits), and delivered — **Week 83 DRAFT v1, 7 pages; Week 84 DRAFT v1, 6 pages — both still awaiting Roop's review as of this catch-up entry.** **Lesson, worth carrying into any future week whose natural topic isn't tied to a fixed developmental point (season, festival, current event, and similar):** don't split it into a separate non-numbered document — keep it in its numbered slot and add an explicit disclaimer telling the reader when the content actually applies and to return to it later if it doesn't apply right now. Investigate the actual app/repository before assuming a delivery-mechanism question needs to go back to Roop — the answer is usually already sitting in the codebase.

**Immediate next step for a new session, superseding the "Immediate next step" note above (which only covered the now-completed Trimester series):** Weeks 83 and 84 are both DRAFT v1, awaiting Roop's review and scoring — apply corrections through as many rounds as needed using the same verification pipeline (pandoc dump → internal A/B duplicate check → cross-week duplicate check against the two immediately preceding locked weeks, exact-line match on A/B option lines only → liver check → LibreOffice-to-PDF → per-page pdftotext inspection → at lock time, a leak check for draft/verification/changelog/score language), then lock both. Only move on to Week 85 when Roop gives a bare "week N" instruction — never propose or ask her to choose a topic; select it independently, checking it against the full theme index above (not just a "Looking ahead" guess) and against the two most recently locked weeks' meal lines for duplication. Before drafting any week built around a developmental age or milestone, compute the implied age as N/4.345 months and confirm it actually matches the intended milestone — see the Week 81 entry above. The working build-script toolchain for this series does not persist between sessions (same caveat as the Trimester series above) and needs to be recreated at `/tmp/nourish/`; the file-naming convention for this series is `Nourish - Postpartum Week N (DRAFT v1/v2/.../LOCKED).docx`, and every locked file also gets copied into `/Users/roop/Downloads/mom-village/` via the device bridge, not just delivered in-conversation.

## Native app — visual polish pass ("Phase 9"), Today tab foundation done, 2026-08-01

After the icon/branding pass, Roop live-tested the native app for the first time since Phase 6-8 landed and gave real, specific feedback: the actual screens look plain/dull compared to the mockups from the original 2026-07-31 full-parity brief (shared again this session since it was in a now-closed chat window), and she found a real bug — the Library reader's Prev/Next buttons sit at a fixed bottom position and collide with the phone's on-screen nav buttons. She confirmed via AskUserQuestion: **native app only** (not the website, which already has its own established design), and **one flow at a time, she reviews each** — starting with the foundation (fonts + the header bug) and Today tab, then Care → Wealth → Library (special focus, see below) → Community → Account in later sessions.

**Real gaps confirmed by reading the actual code, not just eyeballing screenshots:** `constants/theme.ts` already has the exact right brand colors (matches web's tokens exactly), but `Fonts.display`/`Fonts.body` were literally `undefined` — Fraunces/Karla were never loaded natively, so every screen was rendering in the phone's plain system font, which alone explains a lot of the "dull" feeling. `ScreenHeader.tsx` used a fixed `paddingTop: 8` instead of the device's real safe-area inset, which is what caused the wordmark to visibly collide with the phone's status bar/clock in Roop's screenshot. No component anywhere used icon badges, card shadows, or elevation — everything was flat borders and plain text rows, unlike the mockup's rounded icon-badge + soft-shadow card language.

**Built this pass:**
- Added `@expo-google-fonts/fraunces` and `@expo-google-fonts/karla` to `package.json` (verified both packages' exact subpath exports — `@expo-google-fonts/fraunces/600SemiBold`, `/700Bold`, `/600SemiBold_Italic`, `@expo-google-fonts/karla/400Regular`, `/600SemiBold`, `/700Bold` — against the real npm tarballs before writing the imports, not guessed). `app/_layout.tsx` now loads them via `expo-font`'s `useFonts` and gates all rendering behind a loading screen until they're ready, matching the existing auth-loading pattern. `constants/theme.ts`'s `Fonts` object now points at real family names (`Fraunces_600SemiBold` for display, `Karla_400Regular`/`_600SemiBold`/`_700Bold` for body) instead of `undefined`.
- `components/ScreenHeader.tsx` now uses `useSafeAreaInsets()` (confirmed Expo Router's own `ExpoRoot` already wraps the whole app in a `SafeAreaProvider`, so no extra provider setup was needed) instead of a fixed padding value — fixes the status-bar collision for real, not just on the one device it happened to look okay on before.
- `components/CollapsibleCard.tsx` redesigned: takes a new optional `icon` prop, renders it in a colored icon badge, replaced the flat top-border-only card with a real white card + soft shadow/elevation, uses the new Karla semibold font for titles.

Investigated `react-native-page-flipper` (the one real "page curl" package found) and it's a poor fit — low adoption (29 weekly downloads), last real release years old, needs `react-native-linear-gradient` (a separate native module, uncertain Expo Go compatibility), and its whole API renders each page as a single image (built for manga/comic readers) — using it would mean rasterizing every page of every book, a real quality and accessibility regression against the real text content already in the app. Better plan, not yet built: a custom swipe-based page-turn using `react-native-reanimated` + `react-native-gesture-handler`, both already installed dependencies and Expo-Go-safe (no new native module) — a 3D perspective/rotateY transform on swipe, replacing the tap buttons entirely so there's nothing fixed at the bottom to collide with anything.

**Also caught while polishing:** several inline `fontWeight: "700"` styles layered on top of the new custom `fontFamily` values — these are silently ignored on custom (non-variable) font files, since bolding a named font like `Karla_400Regular` requires switching to the actual `Karla_700Bold` family, not adding a weight on top of it. Replaced every instance with the correct bold font family instead, in both the styles objects and a handful of inline `Text` styles (the Maternity Planner's add/subtract labels, PPF/SSY note headers). Worth keeping in mind for every remaining screen in this polish pass — `fontWeight` no longer does anything once a screen has a custom `fontFamily` set.

`app/(tabs)/library.tsx` (bookshelf): Fraunces/Karla throughout, real elevated white cards with shadow replacing flat bordered covers, icon badges on the locked-membership card (library icon) and each series label (cash-outline for the Wealth/finance series, heart-outline for Parenting), "Continue reading" strip restyled to match. Real book cover art was already wired up from a prior phase — this pass was purely the same visual-polish language (fonts, shadows, icons) already applied to Today/Care/Wealth.

`app/library-reader.tsx` (the reader itself) — full rebuild, not just a style pass. The actual bug Roop caught (fixed `paddingTop`/fixed-position bottom Prev/Next buttons colliding with the phone's on-screen nav buttons) is fixed by removing the bottom nav row entirely and replacing it with a real swipe-to-turn gesture, built on `react-native-reanimated` (~4.1.7) + `react-native-gesture-handler` (~2.28) — both already-installed, Expo-Go-safe dependencies confirmed in `package.json`, no new native module added. A `Gesture.Pan()` drives a shared `dragX` value; the current page renders inside an `Animated.View` with a live `perspective`/`rotateY`/`translateX` transform (the page visibly lifts and tilts away in 3D as she drags, with an opacity fade near the end) while the target page renders on a static layer underneath, so something is already there as the current page turns away — a real two-layer page-turn effect, not a flat carousel slide. Releasing past a distance/velocity threshold completes the turn and commits the real page change (`goTo`); releasing early springs back to flat. A small non-interactive "‹ swipe to turn the page ›" hint sits above the safe area (not pinned at the very bottom) for discoverability, replacing the old buttons — nothing is fixed at the screen's bottom edge anymore, which is what directly fixes the collision bug. The reader's top bar also got the same real `useSafeAreaInsets()` fix as every other screen this session, plus icon-ified Bookmark/Contents pills.

Rejected first, before building this: `react-native-page-flipper` (investigated via npm/WebSearch) — low adoption (29 weekly downloads), old/unmaintained, needs a separate `react-native-linear-gradient` native module of uncertain Expo Go compatibility, and its whole API renders each page as a single image (built for manga/comic readers) — using it would mean rasterizing this book's real text content, a real quality/accessibility regression. The custom reanimated/gesture-handler build avoids all three problems.

**Real risk checked before shipping, not assumed:** reanimated v4 moved its worklets engine to a separate `react-native-worklets` package requiring its own babel plugin — and this project has no `babel.config.js` at all. Confirmed by reading `babel-preset-expo`'s own source (`node_modules/expo/node_modules/babel-preset-expo/build/index.js`) that it auto-detects an installed `react-native-worklets` package and injects `react-native-worklets/plugin` automatically with zero config needed — `react-native-worklets` is confirmed present in `node_modules`, so this should transform correctly without Roop needing to touch any build config. Worth remembering if a future gesture/animation feature ever behaves oddly at runtime — check this exact mechanism first.

Verified `npx tsc --noEmit` clean and `npx expo lint` shows zero new errors across both files (`library.tsx` has zero lint issues at all; `library-reader.tsx`'s one flagged line is the same pre-existing "You don't have access to this book yet." apostrophe that existed before this session's edits, confirmed unchanged).

Library's card shadow was flattened too for overall consistency (the reader's own page-turn card shadow was deliberately left alone — it's doing real visual work selling the 3D page-lift effect, not a decorative list card).

**New dependency: `react-native-svg` (15.12.1)**, added to `package.json` for the percentage ring (`Svg`/`Circle` with `strokeDasharray`/`strokeDashoffset`). Confirmed Expo-Go-safe *before* writing any code against it — checked `node_modules/expo/bundledNativeModules.json`, which lists `react-native-svg: 15.12.1` as one of Expo Go's own pre-bundled native modules for this SDK, so it works without a custom dev client, same safety bar as every other native dependency added to this project. Went a step further than usual and actually downloaded the real npm tarball (`npm pack react-native-svg@15.12.1`) and extracted it into `node_modules` in the sandbox to type-check the real `.d.ts` files directly (confirmed `CircleProps`' `cx`/`cy`/`r`/`stroke`/`strokeWidth`/`strokeDasharray`/`strokeDashoffset`/`strokeLinecap`/`transform` prop shapes) rather than guessing at the API — `npx tsc --noEmit` came back fully clean against the real package. `npx expo lint` still shows one `import/no-unresolved` line for this new import — confirmed via `tsc` that it's a resolver-cache artifact of manually extracting the tarball rather than a real `npm install` (which regenerates lockfile/resolver bookkeeping `tsc` doesn't depend on), not a real broken import; expected to clear on its own once Roop runs `npm install`.

**Two things deliberately deferred to the "functionality" pass, flagged rather than silently built as fake-real:** both "See all" links are plain text, not buttons — no "all books" list screen exists yet to send them to. "More to explore" is simply "whatever isn't already in Continue reading" (first 3, catalog order), not a real recommendation/curation feature — reasonable given Roop's explicit sequencing ask, but worth revisiting once she's happy with the look. Real per-book unlock logic (subscription OR individual/bundle purchase) and the reading-progress percentage (computed from the same leaf-index convention the reader itself already uses) are both genuinely real, not placeholders.

**Setup step for Roop:** run `npm install` in `mobile/` again (the new `react-native-svg` dependency needs installing) before `npx expo start`.

**Two real bugs caught live by Roop right after this rebuild, both fixed same session:**

1. **"none of the book can go further than page 4..."** — reported as a reading-progress problem, first ruled out as a content issue by checking the actual bundled JSON files directly (all six books confirmed intact: 306 to 1,617 pages each, nothing truncated) — so this is a reader mechanics bug, not missing content. Two defensive fixes applied to `app/library-reader.tsx`'s swipe gesture, since the exact root cause can't be reproduced in this sandbox (no device/simulator here): (1) the pan gesture's `onUpdate`/`onEnd` worklets were reading `currentRef.current` — a plain ref — directly inside UI-thread worklet code; while Reanimated's declarative Gesture API is documented as safe to rebuild every render, reading a plain ref from inside a worklet is still a real theoretical staleness risk if a given device doesn't rebind the native handler as promptly as expected. Replaced with a proper `currentSV` shared value (`useSharedValue`, kept in sync via a `useEffect` on `current`), which is explicitly designed for safe cross-thread reads — removes the risk category entirely rather than guessing at it. Also fixed a real found bug: `dragDir` wasn't reset to 0 immediately on a *successful* turn (only on cancel), leaving a ~220ms window where a fast second swipe in the same direction could be silently ignored. (2) Added a second, independent way to turn pages regardless of gesture reliability: narrow tap zones at the left/right screen edges, vertically centered (not fixed to the bottom, so this can't reintroduce the original nav-button collision bug), calling `goTo()` directly with no animation dependency. Belt-and-suspenders — the swipe should now work correctly, and even if it doesn't on some device, tapping the edge always will.
2. **"see all button doesn't work.. and all books aree not there"** — real, confirmed gap: "See all" was plain `Text`, not a button, and both shelves (Continue reading / More to explore) only ever show a handful of books at once — the two most recent Parenting titles were never reachable from the bookshelf at all. Fixed with a new screen, **`app/library-all.tsx`** — a real full list of all six books (cover, series label, title, tagline, progress ring or Start/Locked chip), reusing the same unlock logic (subscription/bundle/individual purchase) and the same leaf-index progress-percent calculation as the bookshelf. Both "See all" links now `router.push("/library-all")`. "More to explore" still just shows "whatever isn't in Continue reading" on the main shelf — reasonable now that every book is genuinely one tap away via See all.

Both fixes verified clean on `npx tsc --noEmit` and `npx expo lint` (31 problems, unchanged from the existing pre-session baseline — zero new issues in any touched or new file). **Confirmed working live on Roop's phone, next morning (2026-08-03 session).**

**Follow-up, same live-check: "can we have sub tabs of financial and parenting.. certain mothers are not tech savvy so may not read the obvious.. can we give them sub heads to know these 3 are books about finance and these three for parenting?"** `app/library-all.tsx` restructured to group its flat list into two clearly headed sections — "Wealth & Financial Confidence Series" and "Parenting Series" (reusing `LIBRARY_BOOKS`' own `seriesLabel`/`series` fields, no new copy invented), each with a small icon badge (cash-outline / heart-outline) and a gold-underline header matching the shelf-header language used elsewhere in Library. The row-level tiny series-label caption was removed as redundant once the section header does that job. Deliberately scoped to this screen only, not the main bookshelf — the bookshelf's "Continue reading"/"More to explore" shelves are activity-based groupings (matches Roop's approved mockup), and series sub-heads would conflict with that structure; "All your books" is where a mother browses the full catalog, so it's the right place for series clarity. Verified clean on `tsc`/`eslint` (31 problems, same pre-existing baseline, zero new issues).

Next up: Community, then Account — one screen at a time, each against its own mockup once Roop sends it, per the new page-by-page working agreement. **Not yet seen live on Roop's phone** — this rebuilt Library screen and the reader's swipe/page-turn mechanics both need a first real look once she syncs, since neither could be visually verified in this sandbox.

## Community tab rebuilt — kept as one flat forum, real Orkut-style extras added, 2026-08-02

Roop sent a mockup of a single Community screen (search, "Trending discussion," "Start a discussion," "Browse topics" chips) alongside a long written brief describing how real Orkut Communities worked (separate named sub-communities, owner/moderator/member roles, community creation, privacy settings, ban/approve, polls, notifications, verified badges, AI spam detection). Flagged directly to her that these two documents describe different things — the mockup matches the existing single flat forum, the brief describes a much bigger multi-community platform — and asked her to pick a direction via AskUserQuestion before building anything. She picked **"something in between"**, then confirmed in her own words: **"I don't think we'll need different communities because mom village is a community in itself... we don't need that."** Mom Village stays ONE forum — no separate named sub-communities to create/join (that would have reversed the deliberate "no groups to join" simplification made when Community first shipped, migration_11).

**Schema — `supabase/migration_52_community_pin_lock_likes_polls.sql`.** Pulls in only the pieces of the Orkut brief that genuinely fit a single forum:
- `is_pinned`/`is_locked` booleans on `community_threads` — same "Roop actions this directly in Supabase, no in-app admin panel" pattern as `is_hidden` from migration_22. **To pin a thread:** `update community_threads set is_pinned = true where id = '...';`. **To lock one against new replies:** `update community_threads set is_locked = true where id = '...';`. Both reversible by setting back to `false`.
- Real likes — `like_count` on `community_threads` (denormalized, same pattern as `reply_count`) plus `community_thread_likes` (user_id, thread_id) so a mother can toggle her own like honestly rather than the UI showing a decorative number. Triggers keep the count in sync on insert/delete.
- `user_community_bookmarks` — private per-mother saved-for-later list, same shape as the Library's `user_book_bookmarks` (migration_29).
- Simple polls — `community_polls` (1:1 with a thread, optional), `community_poll_options`, `community_poll_votes` (one vote per mother per poll; voting again changes her vote). Matches the brief's own "Which stroller is best? Nuna / Joie / Chicco" example exactly.

**Explicitly declined from the brief, confirmed with Roop:** separate named sub-communities (Trying to Conceive, Sleep, etc.) and their own creation/ownership/privacy-settings flow; owner/moderator roles beyond what already exists (she moderates directly in Supabase, same as reports); member approval/ban system (nothing to join, so nothing to ban from); a cross-community directory/search-by-category-country-language; notifications (a bell icon appears in her mockup but building real "someone replied" notifications is a genuinely separate, bigger feature — native push itself remains a documented Phase 8 gap); verified pediatrician/lactation-consultant badges; AI-assisted spam detection. None of this was silently dropped — flagged here as a deliberate scope line, not an oversight.

**Native code, all three Community screens rebuilt:**
- `app/(tabs)/community.tsx` — full visual rebuild matching the mockup: a real search bar with an inline icon, a "Trending discussion"/"Recent discussions" section (tapping "See all" toggles the whole list's sort between recent-activity and most-liked — a real sort, not a dead link, learned from the Library "See all" bug the night before), a full-width gold "Start a discussion" button, a "Browse topics" chip grid (all 10 existing topic tags, tapping one filters the list by that tag), and the thread list itself restyled into real cards (avatar-initial circle, name, time-ago, pinned/closed badges, a real heart/like count, reply count, and a bookmark toggle — all live, tappable, optimistically updated, matching this session's "no decorative numbers" bar). Pinned threads always sort to the top regardless of sort mode.
- `app/community-thread.tsx` — pinned/closed badges on the thread itself; a real like + bookmark row; if the thread has a poll, a `PollCard` (vote by tapping an option, percentage bars once she's voted, can change her vote); if the thread `is_locked`, the reply form is replaced entirely with a plain "This discussion has been closed to new replies" notice rather than a fake, disabled-looking form.
- `app/community-new.tsx` — an optional "Add a poll" checkbox revealing a question + 2-5 option inputs (add/remove option rows); on submit, the thread posts first, then the poll (if filled in validly) attaches to it — a poll failing to attach never blocks the thread itself from posting.

All three files restyled with this session's established fonts/card language (`Fonts.*`, `CardStyle`, flat white cards with hairline borders) — the originals still used the phone's system font and `fontWeight` values, never touched in any earlier pass this session since Community hadn't had its turn yet.

Verified clean on `npx tsc --noEmit` and `npx expo lint` (27 problems — caught and fixed two new `no-unused-expressions` warnings from a ternary-as-statement pattern in the like/bookmark toggle handlers before finalizing; remaining 27 are the same pre-existing unescaped-apostrophe baseline in files untouched this pass). **Not yet run in Supabase, not yet seen live on Roop's phone.**

## Account screen rebuilt to match mockup, 2026-08-02

Roop sent a mockup of the Account screen (avatar summary card, Membership/Child profile/Reminder/Privacy/Help rows each with a chevron, a small lotus motif, a reminder row with a toggle switch) and said "yes go ahead" — last screen in the page-by-page pass (Today/Care/Wealth/Library/Community all done before this).

**Real restructure, not just a style pass.** The old `app/account.tsx` was one long scrolling page (inline edit form, inline blocked-members list, inline membership text, inline deletion flow, 7 inline legal links). Split into a summary hub + five drill screens, matching the mockup's tappable-row pattern and reusing `DrillHeader` (per the standing note to reuse it instead of another local header block):
- `app/account.tsx` — rebuilt hub: profile summary card (avatar-initial circle, name, phone — real data, real `router.push("/account-profile")`), then two grouped row-lists: Membership (real Active/Not-subscribed label) → `/account-membership`, Child profile → `/account-child`, a vaccination-reminder row → `/vaccinations`, then Privacy & account deletion → `/account-privacy`, Help → `/account-help`. A small gold flower glyph echoes the mockup's lotus motif. Sign out kept at the bottom (not in the mockup, but removing it would leave no way to sign out).
- `app/account-profile.tsx` (new) — her own name + city + read-only phone, same save logic as before.
- `app/account-child.tsx` (new) — baby's name + DOB/due-date, keeping the exact same deliberate second-confirm-before-saving behavior as the original build (this date drives which chart/schedule she sees).
- `app/account-membership.tsx` (new) — subscription status + the same honest "self-serve cancellation isn't live yet" copy, no fake cancel button.
- `app/account-privacy.tsx` (new) — blocked-members unblock list + the account-deletion request flow, grouped together since both are privacy/safety controls (the mockup doesn't show a separate blocked-list row, so it lives inside this screen instead of adding a 6th top-level row).
- `app/account-help.tsx` (new) — Contact & Help plus the other 6 legal links (About/Privacy/Terms/Guidelines/Refund/Safety), all opening the live website page exactly as before.

**The mockup's Reminder row shows a toggle switch — deliberately NOT built as a real control.** Native push still needs its own APNs/FCM setup (documented Phase 8 gap, unchanged), and this app has a standing rule against ever shipping a toggle that looks live but does nothing (same principle that caught the Library "See all" bug). Instead, the row shows her REAL next-due dose — computed live from the same UIP schedule logic already used on `/vaccinations` (`expandScheduleOccurrences`/`getOccurrenceStatus`/`ageInDays`/`doseLabel`), e.g. "Next vaccination: DPT — Booster 1 · 24 May 2027" — and tapping it goes to the real Vaccinations screen, where the actual due/overdue banner already lives. If she hasn't logged a baby yet or has nothing upcoming, the row just reads "Vaccinations."

Verified clean on `npx tsc --noEmit` and `npx expo lint` (18 problems — down from the prior baseline, since the old `account.tsx`'s own 8 unescaped-apostrophe lines are gone with the rewrite; zero new issues in any of the 6 touched/new files). **This closes the page-by-page mockup pass across all five tabs plus Account. Not yet seen live on Roop's phone.**

## Native app — Phase 8, app icon/branding first-pass design built, 2026-08-01

Picked up the one piece of Phase 8's remainder that doesn't require Roop's own accounts/payment (native push needs APNs/FCM + a Firebase project; store submission needs Apple Developer Program + Google Play Console enrollment — all still genuinely blocked on her, unchanged from the 2026-07-31 audit above). Confirmed with Roop via AskUserQuestion before starting: she picked "app icon/branding first draft" specifically, framed as a first pass for her to react to, not a locked final asset — same draft-then-review pattern as every other piece of content/design in this project.

**Design concept.** A warm doorway/arch (ivory) on the brand's indigo gradient, with a gold "welcoming lamp" dot at its threshold and three small tapering gold dots at the base — a deliberate callback to two motifs already established elsewhere in the app: the homepage hero's arch silhouette, and the three-row gold-dot motif used across the birthday-1/2/3 screens. Colors pulled directly from the locked design tokens (indigo `#1F2E4D`, gold `#D9A441`, gold-deep `#A97418`, ivory `#FBF4E8`) rather than introducing anything new.

**Built as hand-authored SVG, rasterized via `cairosvg`** (no external image-generation tool used — fully vector, reproducible, and easy to tweak if Roop wants color/shape changes rather than needing a full regeneration). Six files replaced in `mobile/assets/images/` (previously Expo's untouched default placeholder assets, flagged as a known gap since Phase 8 was first scoped):
- `icon.png` (1024×1024, opaque RGB, no alpha channel — required for the iOS App Store icon).
- `android-icon-foreground.png` (transparent, the arch/lamp mark sized to fit inside Android's ~66%-diameter adaptive-icon safe zone so it doesn't get clipped by circle/squircle/rounded-square launcher masks).
- `android-icon-background.png` (opaque indigo gradient fill, the adaptive icon's background layer).
- `android-icon-monochrome.png` (white silhouette on transparent, for Android 13+ themed/tinted icons — the OS applies its own tint color).
- `splash-icon.png` — deliberately **not** just the bare ivory arch on transparent: that version visually disappeared when tested against an ivory splash background (caught by rendering a same-color preview before finalizing, not shipped blind). Rebuilt as a self-contained rounded "medallion" badge with its own indigo fill and a gold-deep ring border, so it reads clearly regardless of the page background behind it — verified against both a light (ivory) and dark (indigo) preview composite.
- `favicon.png` (256×256, for the Expo web target only — minor, since this project's real deployment target is native).

**`mobile/app.json` changes** (config only, no logic changed): `android.adaptiveIcon.backgroundColor` changed from Expo's default placeholder light-blue (`#E6F4FE`) to brand indigo `#1F2E4D` (fallback only — the real background comes from `android-icon-background.png`). `expo-splash-screen`'s `backgroundColor` changed from Expo's defaults (white / black) to brand ivory `#FBF4E8` (light mode) / indigo `#1F2E4D` (dark mode); `imageWidth` bumped 200→220 to match the new badge's proportions.

Every render was visually checked before finalizing (not just generated and shipped blind): a 120px simulated home-screen size to confirm it reads clearly at real icon scale, a circular-crop preview to check nothing important gets clipped by Android's launcher mask, and light/dark splash composites. Asset-only + config change, nothing else touched — safe to commit via GitHub Desktop whenever Roop's ready, no migration needed, no `tsc`/`eslint` impact.

**Not done / explicitly still open:** this hasn't been seen on an actual device or simulator yet — this sandbox can't run a full Expo build, so it's only been verified as static renders, not inside the real app shell (splash-screen timing, exact adaptive-icon mask on a real Android launcher, etc.). Worth a look via Expo Go once Roop's synced the new assets. If she wants a different concept entirely rather than refining this one, that's a cheap redo since it's all hand-authored SVG, not a locked external design file. The rest of Phase 8 — real APNs/FCM push, Apple Developer Program enrollment ($99/yr), Google Play Console enrollment ($25), and the EAS build — remains fully blocked on Roop's own accounts and payment, unchanged from the 2026-07-31 status above.

## Vercel production deploys were silently broken for ~14 hours, 2026-08-01 — fixed

Discovered while confirming the Phase 6-8 push had actually gone live: it hadn't. `tsconfig.json`'s `include` pattern (`**/*.tsx`, `**/*.ts`) and `eslint.config.mjs` had no exclusion for `mobile/` — so every `next build` on Vercel was type-checking and linting the native Expo app's files too, and failing immediately (React Native/Expo imports like `expo-image-picker` don't resolve against the website's own `node_modules`). This had been silently failing since the very first native-app commit (`20ff601`, "Add native app v1 + Phase 1 layout rebuild") — every real commit after that (`fdef40d` Bearer-token auth, `91b574d` Phases 6-8) built and errored in ~31 seconds, and Production stayed frozen on a stale build from before the native app existed. Not a cosmetic issue as first logged (see the old Razorpay-session note below) — this meant roughly 14 hours of real web fixes, including the Bearer-token auth support the native app's vaccination/memory screens depend on, were never actually live.

**Fix:** added `"mobile"` to `tsconfig.json`'s `exclude` array, and `"mobile/**"` to `eslint.config.mjs`'s `globalIgnores`. Verified locally: `npx tsc --noEmit` and `npx eslint .` both clean from the repo root (previously threw `Cannot find module 'expo-image-picker'` etc.). `next build` gets past that step now too — this sandbox still can't complete a full build due to Google Fonts being unreachable from its network proxy (pre-existing, unrelated, doesn't affect Vercel's real build environment).

Pushed as `db472c5` ("Exclude mobile/ from Next.js type-check and lint (fixes broken production deploys)"). The auto-deploy webhook didn't pick it up for over an hour after push (separate, still-unexplained flakiness — worth watching for on future pushes), so it was deployed manually via Vercel's Deployments → "..." → **Create Deployment** → pasted `main` branch → **Deploy to Production**. **Confirmed Ready and Production, 2026-08-01.** This also brings `fdef40d` (Bearer-token auth) live for the first time, since it was stuck behind this same broken-build chain — the native app's vaccination-card-reading and memory transcription/recall screens should now actually work end to end.

**Worth doing if this happens again:** check Settings → Git Settings in the Vercel dashboard to confirm the GitHub webhook is actually firing (a build appearing in the Deployments list at all, even as an Error, means the webhook fired; total silence for a pushed commit — as happened with `db472c5` for over an hour — means it didn't, and needs a manual "Create Deployment" like this one).

## Native app — Phase 8 (Polish + store prep) — product-rules audit done, remainder blocked on Roop's own accounts, 2026-07-31

Eighth and final phase off the agreed plan. Unlike Phases 2-7, this phase is genuinely NOT fully buildable by Claude alone — three of its four pieces require Roop's own real-world accounts, payments, and identity verification (Apple Developer Program, Google Play Console, a Firebase project for FCM, APNs certificates) that no sandbox session can create or fake. What was actually completed this pass, and what's left for her:

**Done: product-rules audit.** Checked the brief's three explicit product rules — no guilt-based notifications, no endless feed, no clutter — against every native screen built across Phases 1-7. Searched for guilt/streak/shame/urgency language, badge/unread counters, infinite-scroll feed patterns, and gamified "days in a row" tracking. Result: clean. The one matching hit for "guilt" is in `wealth-savings.tsx`'s scam-awareness content itself (warning mothers to be cautious of anything *sold* to them using guilt) — the opposite of a violation. Community's thread list (`app/(tabs)/community.tsx`) is a bounded search-result `FlatList`, not an algorithmic auto-loading feed. Care's mood/time check-in state (`app/(tabs)/care.tsx`) has no streak or score exposed to her, matching the "gentle, non-punitive, never a streak/score" standard already locked for the Care Chart on web. No push-notification code exists anywhere yet (expected — see below), so there's nothing to audit there until it's actually built.

**Not done, genuinely blocked on Roop, in order of what's needed first:**
1. **Real native push notifications (APNs for iOS, FCM for Android)** — needs an Apple Developer Program account ($99/yr) to generate an APNs key/certificate, and a Firebase project (free) to configure FCM, before any code can be written against them. `expo-notifications` is the library to add once those exist; the web app's existing `user_push_subscriptions`/`sendPushToUser()` pattern (migration_23) is the model to extend, not replace — one push abstraction, three delivery channels (web, iOS, Android) sharing the same trigger logic (vaccination due-dates today; Care Chart/Monthly Chart nudges are still-unbuilt options flagged since the web push section).
2. **App icon + splash/branding artwork** — currently Expo's default placeholder assets (`assets/images/icon.png` etc., untouched since scaffolding). Needs real brand artwork sized to each platform's exact icon/splash spec — a design task, not a code task; worth a dedicated pass once Roop has real assets or wants Claude to generate a first-pass design to react to.
3. **Apple Developer Program enrollment** ($99/yr, register as Individual not Organization — faster approval, per the original v1 framework decision) — needed before any TestFlight build or App Store submission.
4. **Google Play Console enrollment** ($25 one-time) — needed before any Play Store submission.
5. **A real EAS (Expo Application Services) build** — once 1-4 exist, `eas build` produces the actual installable `.ipa`/`.aab` files for store submission; this sandbox can't run that build (needs Roop's Apple/Google credentials wired into an EAS project) or install/test the result on a real device beyond what Expo Go already allows.

**What this means practically:** all 8 phases of the agreed plan are now either fully built (Phases 1-7) or have reached the point where the only remaining work requires Roop's own accounts and real money (Phase 8's remainder). This is a natural, deliberate stopping point for the phased build — not an oversight. Nothing above blocks her from continuing to use/test the app via Expo Go exactly as she has been.

**Status: Phase 8's buildable piece (product-rules audit) done, 2026-07-31. The rest of Phase 8 needs Roop's own developer-account setup before any further native-app code can be written against it.** This closes out the 8-phase native build plan agreed 2026-07-31 — full review is Roop's own next step, per her original framing ("I will analyze it in the end"). **Pushed via GitHub Desktop 2026-07-31** as part of the Phase 6-8 commit (`91b574d`).

Seventh phase off the agreed 8-phase plan. ## Native app — Phase 6 (Vaccinations + Memories) built and synced, 2026-07-31

Sixth phase off the agreed 8-phase plan. Built the two bounded 0-3 additions — vaccination tracking and voice/photo memories + recall — replacing the two placeholder-free gaps left after Phase 1 (these were never given their own tab, same as web, since they're deliberately bounded additions, not a 6th pillar). Reuses `user_vaccination_records`, `user_voice_logs`, `user_photo_logs`, plus the `vaccination-cards`/`voice-logs`/`memory-photos` private Storage buckets exactly as they exist for web (migrations 12/18). No new tables needed.

**Real architectural first for this project: a web-side code change was required to support the native app.** Every phase before this one was purely additive on the native side — direct Supabase table queries, zero web changes. This phase's two AI-backed features (Claude Vision reading a vaccination card, Whisper transcribing a voice note, Claude answering a recall question) all run through the *website's existing Next.js API routes* (`/api/vaccination/extract`, `/api/memories/transcribe`, `/api/memories/recall`) rather than duplicating server-side secrets into the mobile app — but those routes were cookie-auth-only (`@supabase/ssr`), which doesn't exist for a native app with no browser cookies. Fixed with a new shared helper, **`src/lib/supabase/apiAuth.ts`** (`getAuthedSupabase(req)`): if the request carries an `Authorization: Bearer <token>` header, it validates that token directly against Supabase; otherwise it falls through to the exact original cookie-based path, completely unchanged for every existing web request. All three routes were updated to use this helper in place of the old cookie-only client. Verified via `npx tsc --noEmit` and `npx eslint` on the whole repo (zero new errors) plus a `git stash` check confirming one pre-existing, unrelated FormData type error in `transcribe/route.ts` predates this change and isn't a regression.

**Built:**
- `mobile/lib/api.ts` — `authedFetch(path, init)`, a thin wrapper that reads the native Supabase session's access token and attaches it as a Bearer header before calling `https://www.momvillage.in<path>` — the one place the native app calls back into the website's own server instead of Supabase directly.
- `mobile/lib/vaccinationSchedule.ts` — verbatim port of the web UIP schedule/logic (`expandScheduleOccurrences`, `getOccurrenceStatus`, `ageInDays`, `doseLabel`, `occurrenceOptions`), pure TS, no browser dependency either side.
- `mobile/app/vaccinations.tsx` — schedule list with the same overdue/due-soon/upcoming/given status logic and banner as web's `dashboard/vaccinations/page.tsx`, gated on subscription + baby already born.
- `mobile/app/vaccinations-log.tsx` — photo (camera or library, via `expo-image-picker`) → sent to `/api/vaccination/extract` via `authedFetch` → AI vaccine/date suggestion shown for her review (never auto-saved) → she picks the actual dose from a dropdown and confirms/edits the date → card photo uploaded to the `vaccination-cards` bucket → upserted into `user_vaccination_records`, same shape as web.
- `mobile/app/memories.tsx` — three pieces in one screen, same as web's `MemoriesClient.tsx`: a recall question box (`authedFetch` to `/api/memories/recall`); voice recording via `expo-av`'s `Audio.Recording` API → `authedFetch` multipart POST to `/api/memories/transcribe` → review/edit transcript before saving to `user_voice_logs`; photo logging via `expo-image-picker` → uploaded to the `memory-photos` bucket → saved to `user_photo_logs` with an optional caption; a merged, reverse-chronological timeline of both log types with signed photo URLs (`createSignedUrls`, 1hr).
- `mobile/app.json` — added `expo-image-picker` (camera + photo-library permission strings) and `expo-av` (microphone permission string) as config plugins.
- `mobile/package.json` — added `expo-image-picker@~17.0.11`, `expo-av@~16.0.8`, `expo-file-system@~19.0.23` (exact SDK-54-compatible versions, read directly from `node_modules/expo/bundledNativeModules.json` since this sandbox's network proxy blocks the `npx expo install` compatibility check — same recurring workaround as every prior phase's dependency installs).
- `mobile/app/(tabs)/index.tsx` — Today tab's "Track vaccinations" and "Log a memory" quick actions now route to the new native screens (`router.push("/vaccinations")` / `"/memories"`) instead of opening the website in the phone's browser. "Past months" still opens the website — the native archive view isn't built (out of this phase's scope; nothing currently plans to build it, since Today already shows the current month and past months are a lower-value native target).

**One real SDK-54 API gotcha caught by `tsc`, not silently shipped:** `expo-file-system` 19.x (this SDK's version) replaced the old `readAsStringAsync`/`EncodingType` API with a new File/Directory-class API by default — the old API still exists, but only under an `expo-file-system/legacy` subpath import. `memories.tsx`'s photo-upload base64 read uses this legacy subpath explicitly, with a comment explaining why, rather than fighting the new API for a one-off base64 read.

Verified clean on `npx tsc --noEmit`. Built and checked in the sandbox's native filesystem, then synced into the mounted `mobile/` folder — `diff -rq` confirmed byte-identical across `app/`, `lib/`, `package.json`, and `app.json` after the sync. Not yet pushed via GitHub Desktop (native side) or via GitHub Desktop (the three web route files + new `apiAuth.ts` helper) — same "she pushes when ready" pattern as every other change in this project. **Both the mobile and web changes need to go out together** — the native screens won't work until the website's updated API routes are actually deployed.

**Known gaps, flagged rather than silently skipped:** no native due-date push reminder for vaccinations yet (native push is Phase 8). No native "past months" vaccination history view — only the current schedule. Voice recording uses `expo-av`, which Expo has begun deprecating in favor of `expo-audio`/`expo-video` in newer SDKs — fine on SDK 54 (still the actively-supported store-installable version per the SDK-mismatch note above), worth revisiting on a future SDK bump.

**Status: Phase 6 built and synced, 2026-07-31. Pushed via GitHub Desktop 2026-07-31** — both the web routes (`fdef40d`, "Add Bearer-token auth support for native app API calls") and the native side (`91b574d`, "Native app Phases 6-8...") are live in `main`. Next: Phase 7 (Account + trust/legal — full account screen rebuild, child profile editing, blocked-members unblock UI, privacy/deletion request, help, plus About/Contact/Privacy/Terms/Guidelines/Refund/Safety links).

## Native app — Phase 5 (Community tab) built and synced, 2026-07-31

Fifth phase off the agreed 8-phase plan. Built the real Community tab — flat Orkut-style forum, full-text search, starter-prompt empty state, structured topic tags, replies, and report/block controls — replacing the Phase 1 placeholder. No schema changes needed; reuses `community_threads`, `community_replies`, `community_author_names`, `user_blocks`, and `community_reports` exactly as they exist for web (migrations 11/22/30).

Refetches on screen focus (`useFocusEffect`) so returning from posting a thread or reply shows the update immediately, same effect as web's `router.refresh()`.

Posts straight to `community_threads` and routes into the new thread.

Block upserts into `user_blocks` and immediately filters that member's replies out of the current view; the reciprocal "Blocked members" management list (already on web's account page) stays a Phase 7 (Account) item — for now a blocked member's content just stops showing, reversal isn't reachable natively yet.

Verified clean on `npx tsc --noEmit`. Built and checked in the sandbox's native filesystem, then synced into the mounted `mobile/` folder — `diff -rq` confirmed byte-identical across `app/`, `lib/`, `components/`, `constants/` after the sync. Not yet pushed via GitHub Desktop.

A mother can still block from either platform since `user_blocks` is the same table, she just can't undo it from the app until then (she could still ask Roop, or use the website in the meantime).

**Status: Phase 5 built and synced, 2026-07-31. Not yet pushed via GitHub Desktop — same "she pushes when ready" pattern as every other change.** Next: Phase 6 (Vaccinations + Memories).

## Native app — Phase 4 (Library tab) built and synced, 2026-07-31

Fourth phase off the agreed 8-phase plan. Built the real Library tab — bookshelf plus an in-app reader for all six locked, already-written books — replacing the Phase 1 placeholder. No schema changes needed; reuses `user_reading_progress`, `user_book_bookmarks`, and `user_book_purchases` exactly as they exist for web (migrations 21/29, plus the Razorpay purchases table).

**The one deliberate, previously-flagged gap: no physical page-flip animation.** Web's `react-pageflip` is browser-only with no React Native equivalent — flagged as an accepted difference when the full-parity brief was first reviewed. The native reader is still calm and immersive (cover page, real chapter breaks, table of contents, bookmarks, a progress bar, "picking up where you left off") with a plain Prev/Next button turn instead of a page-curl effect.

**Built:**
- `mobile/content/library/*.json` — all six books' paginated content, copied verbatim (byte-for-byte) from the website's own `src/content/library/` — same docx → HTML → paginated-JSON pipeline, no re-conversion. ~3.7MB total across six books, bundled directly into the app via `require()` (Metro resolves JSON imports synchronously, unlike web's async `import()`).
- `mobile/types/library-content.ts` — verbatim port of the shared page/block types.
- `mobile/lib/library.ts` — book metadata (title/tagline/series), ported from web's version with one adjustment: cover images stay hosted on the live website (`https://www.momvillage.in/library-covers/*.png`) rather than bundled into the app, since they're already public static assets there and this avoids ~400KB of duplicate image weight in the app bundle.
- `mobile/lib/libraryContent.ts` — a sync per-slug content loader (`loadBookContent`), the sync equivalent of web's `CONTENT_LOADERS` dynamic-import map.
- `mobile/lib/purchases.ts` — verbatim port of `hasPurchasedBook`/`hasPurchasedBundle`/`hasPurchasedBudgetMap` (pure Supabase queries, no browser dependency).
- `mobile/app/(tabs)/library.tsx` — the real bookshelf: Wealth and Parenting series grids, a "Continue reading" strip (from `user_reading_progress`, most-recently-read first, capped at 3), and per-book unlock logic (subscription OR individual/bundle purchase). **No in-app purchase flow** — per this project's standing no-native-IAP decision, a locked book's "Buy for ₹249" badge and the bundle CTA both open the web Library via `Linking.openURL` rather than processing payment in-app.
- `mobile/app/library-reader.tsx` — the reader itself, a pushed route taking `?slug=`. Renders one page at a time (cover / chapter content with real block types — paragraph, heading, list, callout box, table / end page) with Prev/Next buttons, a bookmark toggle (★, saving to `user_book_bookmarks`), a Contents modal (chapters + her bookmarks, tap to jump), a progress bar, and debounced (700ms) autosave into `user_reading_progress` — identical page-index semantics to web (0 = cover, 1..N = content, N+1 = end), so a mother's reading position stays in sync whichever platform she's on.

Verified clean on `npx tsc --noEmit` (JSON imports resolve fine under Expo's default `resolveJsonModule` tsconfig). Built and checked in the sandbox's native filesystem, then synced into the mounted `mobile/` folder — `diff -rq` confirmed byte-identical across `app/`, `lib/`, `content/`, `types/`, `components/`, `constants/` after the sync. Not yet pushed via GitHub Desktop.

**Status: Phase 4 built and synced, 2026-07-31. Not yet pushed via GitHub Desktop — same "she pushes when ready" pattern as every other change.** Next: Phase 5 (Community tab).

## Razorpay KYC — approved via the unregistered-business path, 2026-07-31

Big unblock: the KYC "Upload Additional Document" stall from 2026-07-30 (no Shop Establishment Certificate/Import Export Certificate/postpaid mobile bill) is resolved. Razorpay's own team called Roop and pointed her to their **unregistered/individual business** onboarding path instead of continuing the registered-Proprietorship path — no additional document needed that way. Walked her through it live, screen by screen:

- **Business category**: their AI category-picker chat asked a few questions (digital vs. physical, B2C vs. B2B, education-content vs. financial-services framing). Answered to keep her clearly in **Education > E-learning** — matches her existing Udyam category and correctly avoids a "financial services" classification (Wealth is one section among several, general education only, not advice — same line this project has held throughout).
- **Business model checkboxes**: only "We provide recorded sessions that students can access anytime" checked (closest fit for self-paced, on-demand digital content) — third-party Edtech platform/physical materials/offline coaching all correctly left unchecked.

## Native app — full-parity brief received, phased build plan set, 2026-07-31

Roop live-tested the v1 slice (see below) and gave feedback that reopens the earlier "phased rollout, Wealth/Library/Community deferred to v2/v3" decision: **the native app must eventually have everything the website has, full parity, before launch — nothing left behind.** She provided a detailed product/layout brief (drafted with ChatGPT, mockups included) specifying a five-tab nav (Today/Care/Wealth/Library/Community, with Account/reminders/help/policies moved behind a profile icon rather than its own tab), a four-screen native onboarding flow, and full screen-by-screen specs for every pillar. Her explicit instruction: build in phases, sequence is Claude's call, but the nav/structure should be built right from the start so it isn't redesigned again as later phases land.

Two things flagged back to her as real (not cosmetic) differences from the brief, both acknowledged, neither blocking:
1. **The Library reader** — the website's page-flip animation (`react-pageflip`) is a browser-only library with no React Native equivalent. The native reader will still be calm/immersive with table of contents, bookmarks, and "continue where you left off," but won't have an identical page-turn physical effect.
2. **Push notification reminders** (shown as a working toggle in her Account mockup) — the website only has **web push** built (migration_23, `src/lib/push.ts`). Native push needs its own infrastructure (APNs for iOS, FCM for Android) that hasn't been started — this toggle will need to be a "coming soon" placeholder until that's built, not a working switch, in whichever phase touches Account.

**Agreed phase plan (Claude's sequencing, confirmed by Roop):**
1. **Foundation** — restructure to the real 5-tab nav + profile icon; rebuild Today (priorities strip, quick actions, 6 collapsible chart sections) and Care (landing screen, 3-question check-in, 5-card daily chart) to match the brief's mockups exactly. Everything else depends on this frame being right first.
2. **Native onboarding** — Welcome → About you → What matters most → First preview, so a brand-new mobile-only signup no longer gets punted to the website.
3. **Wealth tab** — checklist, Minimum Budget Planner (real interactive version), Government Benefits & Savings Directory, Savings & Financial Planning Guidance.
4. **Library tab** — bookshelf + native reader (see caveat above).
5. **Community tab** — search, trending, start-discussion with topic chips, replies, report/block — same Supabase tables as web.
6. **Vaccinations + Memories** — dedicated screens, reusing the existing photo-upload/AI-extraction and voice/photo-logging backend work.
7. **Account + trust/legal** — full account screen + About/Contact/Privacy/Terms/Guidelines/Refund/Safety links.
8. **Polish + store prep** — real native push notifications, app icon/branding, a pass against the brief's product rules (no guilt-based notifications, no endless feed, no clutter), then Apple/Google store accounts and submission.

**Status: Phase 1 built and synced, same session, 2026-07-31.** Full mockups and brief text are in the chat transcript, not re-copied into this file — refer back to that conversation for exact screen-by-screen wording/visuals if a future session needs to re-check something against the original brief.

## Native app — Expo Go SDK mismatch fixed, 2026-07-31 (same evening as the v1 build below)

While walking Roop through actually running the v1 build live on her phone (Expo Go install → `npm install` → `npx expo start` → scan QR), the app failed to load with **"Project is incompatible with this version of Expo Go."** Root cause, confirmed via Expo's own changelog before touching anything: Apple/Google have been slow approving new Expo Go releases through 2026 (a public, ongoing issue — Expo's own May 2026 and June 30 2026 (SDK 57 launch) posts both confirm this), so the real Expo Go app installable from the Play Store/App Store right now is capped at an older SDK. The v1 build below was scaffolded on SDK 57 (whatever `create-expo-app` pulled as "latest" that day) — several versions ahead of what the store's Expo Go build actually supports, hence the rejection.

**Fix: re-scaffolded the whole project on Expo SDK 54** (`create-expo-app@latest --template default@sdk-54`) — SDK 54 is explicitly confirmed in Expo's own changelog as continuously available on both app stores throughout this whole approval slowdown, so it's the safe, boring choice rather than gambling on whether 55/56 have since cleared review. All of the v1 code (Supabase client, auth screens, Monthly Chart, Care Chart, Account) ported over with **zero logic changes** — it's plain React Native + Supabase, nothing SDK-57-specific was ever used. Only structural difference: this SDK 54 template's default layout is a flat `app/`/`lib/`/`constants/` at the project root (no `src/` wrapper the SDK 57 template used), so file paths moved up one level — the relative import paths inside every file stayed identical by coincidence (same relative nesting depth either way). Re-verified clean on `npx tsc --noEmit` and `npx expo-doctor` (16/18 checks pass; the 2 failures are network-only checks blocked by this sandbox's proxy, not real project issues) after the port.

**Worth remembering for any future Expo SDK upgrade on this project:** before bumping the `expo` package version, check `expo.dev/changelog` for whether the store-installable Expo Go actually supports that SDK yet — the newest SDK from `create-expo-app@latest` is not guaranteed to match what a mother can actually download and run today.

## Native app — v1 slice built, 2026-07-31

Following on from the 2026-07-30 decision below to start native development. Four structural questions were resolved via AskUserQuestion before any code was written (Roop deferred to Claude's recommendation on all four):

1. **Framework: React Native + Expo** (Expo Router). Chosen over Flutter specifically because it shares TypeScript and the Supabase JS SDK with the existing Next.js web app — Flutter would mean zero code/mental-model sharing for a solo-founder-plus-Claude team.
2. **Rollout: phased, not a full 5-pillar rebuild.** V1 = auth + Monthly Chart + Care Chart. Wealth/Library/Community wait for v2/v3 — Library needs a real in-app reader, Community carries extra App Store/Play Store scrutiny as user-generated content.
3. **Payments: no in-app purchases in the app at all, in any version yet.** Apple/Google require their own in-app purchase systems for digital subscriptions bought inside a native app — embedding Razorpay checkout natively is a real rejection risk. Instead: the app is access-only. A mother who already subscribed on web just logs in and it works. A mother without a subscription is sent to momvillage.in in the system browser to actually pay — no in-app purchase flow built, no store-review risk. Revisit this only if Roop later wants true native IAP as a deliberate phase 2/3 decision.
4. **Dev accounts/devices: none yet, none needed to start.** Expo Go (free) lets Roop run the app live on her own phone during development with zero developer account. Apple Developer Program ($99/yr, register as Individual not Organization — faster approval) and Google Play Console ($25 one-time) are only needed near actual store submission.

**Repo structure:** the native app lives at `mobile/` inside this same `mom-village` repo/folder (not a separate repo) — so Roop manages it through the exact same GitHub Desktop workflow she already knows, just one more folder to commit/push.

**What was built, all verified clean on `npx tsc --noEmit`:**
- Scaffolded via `create-expo-app` (Expo SDK 57, Expo Router, TypeScript). The default template's demo screens/components (the "@expo/ui" native-tabs demo, animated splash icon, etc.) were stripped out in favor of the standard, stable `Tabs` navigator from `expo-router` — the template shipped with `expo-router/unstable-native-tabs`, which is explicitly experimental and not worth the risk for a foundation meant to last.
- `src/lib/supabase.ts` — same Supabase project as web (`EXPO_PUBLIC_SUPABASE_URL`/`EXPO_PUBLIC_SUPABASE_ANON_KEY`, read from `mobile/.env` — already filled in with the real values, same ones as the website's `.env.local`, so it works immediately without Roop doing anything). Session persistence via `@react-native-async-storage/async-storage`.
- `src/lib/monthCalculator.ts`, `weekCalculator.ts`, `subscription.ts`, `season.ts` — ported verbatim from the web app's equivalents (pure date/logic functions, no browser dependency either side, so copy-paste is genuinely safe here — flagged in each file's header comment to keep both copies in sync if the logic ever changes).
- `src/lib/AuthContext.tsx` + `src/app/_layout.tsx` — session-aware root layout using the standard Expo Router auth-guard pattern (redirect to `/login` if no session and trying to reach the app).
- `src/app/login.tsx` — phone/OTP screen, same validation and Supabase calls as `src/app/login/page.tsx` on web.
- `src/app/onboarding-needed.tsx` — placeholder for a genuinely new mobile-only signup with no profile yet (native onboarding isn't built this pass — she's told to finish onboarding on the website, then the same account picks up in the app automatically).
- `src/app/(tabs)/index.tsx` — Monthly Chart, querying the same `monthly_chart_content` table (delivery type/season branching, real saving checkboxes into `user_monthly_chart_progress`), locked-preview message + link to web for non-subscribers.
- `src/app/(tabs)/care.tsx` — Care Chart: daily check-in (time/mood, writes `user_daily_checkin`) then that week's matched content from `care_chart_week_content` (Move tier by time, Reset message by mood, condition notes filtered by her `health_flags`). Works for literally every week (1-196) since that table now covers the full pregnancy-through-third-birthday span.
- `src/app/(tabs)/account.tsx` — profile summary (phone, not email — same standing note as web, this app has no email field), membership status, sign out.

**V1 scope cuts, flagged plainly (not oversights):** Care tab renders read-only (no per-card "done" toggle into `user_care_week_progress` yet, unlike Monthly Chart's real checkboxes). No diet-preference protein-tip layer. No app icon/splash screen artwork yet (using Expo's placeholder assets). No linting set up (`tsc` is clean; `eslint`/`expo lint` wasn't wired up this pass). Native onboarding for a brand-new mobile-only mother isn't built (see `onboarding-needed.tsx` above).

**A build-process note for future sessions, in case this recurs:** this sandbox's mounted `mom-village` folder is a FUSE bridge to Roop's real Mac, and (like the Razorpay `npm install` issue from 2026-07-30) `npm install`/large rsync operations directly against it can fail with `ENOTEMPTY` rename errors or silent permission issues on delete. Building the Expo project directly inside the mounted folder failed this way. Worked around by scaffolding the whole app in the sandbox's own native filesystem (`$HOME`, not under any mounted path), running `npm install`/`tsc` there where it's reliable, then copying only the source files (never `node_modules`) into the mounted `mobile/` folder at the end. **Real mistake made and corrected this session:** an early `rsync` copy accidentally included `node_modules` and a leftover pre-cleanup template file in the mounted folder; Roop was asked before anything was deleted, approved it, and it was removed — nothing under `src/`, `supabase/`, or any existing website file was ever touched.

**Setup Roop needs to do, whenever she's ready to actually see it running:**
1. Open `mobile/` in a terminal (or ask Claude to run it for her, once a workspace session has terminal/device access), run `npm install`.
2. `.env` is already filled in with the real Supabase values — nothing to do there.
3. `npx expo start`, scan the QR code with the free **Expo Go** app on her phone. No developer account needed for this step.
4. Apple Developer ($99/yr, Individual account) and Google Play Console ($25 one-time) only get set up later, near actual store submission — not needed to keep developing.

**Not built yet:** Wealth, Library, Community pillars on mobile (later phases per the phased-rollout decision); native onboarding flow; app icon/splash branding; any native push notification setup (web push already exists per the section below, native would need APNs/FCM separately); linting.
